({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{({\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\alpha\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\beta]\hskip 0.4875pt\hskip 0.73125pt{{\lceil}\hskip 0.975pt{\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\delta\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.4875ptM}\hskip 0.975pt{\rfloor}}})\hskip 0.4875ptN}\colon{B}\mathrel{\mid}{\beta\mathord{:}C,\Delta}\hskip 0.4875pt

(\textsl{Wk})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{M}\colon{A\mathord{\rightarrow}B}\mathrel{\mid}{\delta\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}D,\beta\mathord{:}C,\Delta}\hskip 0.44054pt

(\textsl{Wk})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{N}\colon{A}\mathrel{\mid}{\delta\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}D,\beta\mathord{:}C,\Delta}\hskip 0.42952pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{{M}\hskip 0.45183ptN}\colon{B}\mathrel{\mid}{\delta\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}D,\beta\mathord{:}C,\Delta}\hskip 0.45183pt

(\mu)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.2317pt\delta\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.46342pt{M}\hskip 0.46342ptN}\colon{D}\mathrel{\mid}{\delta\mathord{:}B,\beta\mathord{:}C,\Delta}\hskip 0.46342pt

(\mu)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\gamma\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\beta]\hskip 0.4875pt\hskip 0.73125pt{{\lceil}\hskip 0.975pt{\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\delta\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.4875pt{M}\hskip 0.4875ptN}\hskip 0.975pt{\rfloor}}}\colon{B}\mathrel{\mid}{\beta\mathord{:}C,\Delta}\hskip 0.4875pt

Notice that these are the ‘inhabited’ version of the proofs in Example
2.3
; remember that a
({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.4pt})
-
({\textsl{Act}\hskip 0.4pt})
pair collapses into
(\mu)
.
The derivation
D_{2}^{\prime}
is in structure equal to
D_{2}
, since that is decided by the syntactic structure of the context
\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.75pt{{\lceil}\hskip 1.0pt{\mathop{\cdot}}\hskip 1.0pt{\rfloor}}
but contains
\mu\hskip 0.25pt\delta\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.5pt{M}\hskip 0.5ptN
rather than
\mu\hskip 0.25pt\delta\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5ptM
.

The intuition behind the structural rule is given by de Groote
[
dG94
]
: “
in a
{\lambda\mu}
-term
\mu\alpha.M
of type
A\mathop{\rightarrow}B
, only the subterms named by
\alpha
are
really
of type
A\mathop{\rightarrow}B
(…); hence, when such a
\mu
-abstraction is applied to an argument, this argument must be passed over to the sub-terms named by
\alpha
.
”
Remark that this is accurate, but hides the fact that the naming construction
[\alpha]M
is actually a (hidden) instance of rule
({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
, so ‘naming’ is actually a kind of application.
The proof of Peirce’s Law (Example
2.2
) can be inhabited in
{\lambda\mu}
with
\lambda x.\mu\alpha.[\alpha](x(\lambda y.\mu\beta.[\alpha]y))
.
In
[
Par92
]
, Parigot shows that ‘double negation elimination’ can be represented in
{\lambda\mu}
; as suggested above,
{\perp}
is added as a pseudo-type to express negation
\neg A
through
A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}
, as well as contradiction.

Example 2.11

(Double negation elimination in
{\lambda\mu}
)
.

Double negation elimination is shown in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
by the proof on the left;
we can also show this in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}
, as in the proof on the right, but since
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}
has no rules for negation, we need to add
{\perp}
to express it, so write
C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}
for
\neg C
.

\begin{array}[]{c@{\dquad}c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 37.8529pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=75.25398pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.90367pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\neg\neg C,\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}\neg\neg C$}}}}\>\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 33.6806pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=66.92067pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.88107pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\neg\neg C,\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}\neg C$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=204.48912pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.92685pt${\mbox{$({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 73.13211pt\hbox{$\neg\neg C,\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{\perp}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=236.46793pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.95062pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{PbC}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 98.9826pt\hbox{$\neg\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}C$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=274.17493pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 110.44725pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\vdash}\neg\neg C\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\Rightarrow\hskip 1.0pt}C$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\put(30.0,0.0){.}\put(30.0,50.0){.}\put(30.0,100.0){.}\put(30.0,150.0){.}\put(30.0,200.0){.}\put(30.0,250.0){.}\put(30.0,300.0){.}\raise 43.5pt\hbox to28.45274pt{\kern-56.9055pt
\hskip 5.11108pt{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 49.19005pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.80556pt,depth=-2.30553pt,width=97.92827pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.90367pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\vdash}{(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{C}\hskip 0.45183pt$}}}}\>\penalty}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 38.93228pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=77.43504pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.85904pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp},C}\mathrel{\vdash}{C}\mathrel{\mid}{C}\hskip 0.42952pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=108.38345pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.88107pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.35242pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 15.64993pt\hbox{${(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp},C}\mathrel{\vdash}{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{C}\hskip 0.44054pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=146.31186pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.90367pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.90367pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 34.33069pt\hbox{${(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\vdash}{C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{C}\hskip 0.45183pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=223.89366pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.92685pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 79.54704pt\hbox{${(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\vdash}{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{C}\hskip 0.46342pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=254.2058pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.95062pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Act}\hskip 0.38023pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 98.44716pt\hbox{${(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\vdash}{C}\mathrel{\mid}{}\hskip 0.47531pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=288.54301pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 105.47076pt\hbox{${}\mathrel{\vdash}{((C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}C}\mathrel{\mid}{{\perp}}\hskip 0.4875pt$}}}}\end{array}

Notice that the rule
({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.4pt})
is not directly followed by
({\textsl{Act}\hskip 0.4pt})
, while they always come together in
{\lambda\mu}
, and that the assumption
\neg\neg C,\neg C\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}\neg C
gets replaced by the proof for
{(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}{C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{C}\hskip 0.5pt
.
Moreover,
({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
is represented through
({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 1.0pt})
and
({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
.

Parigot shows that double negation elimination can be represented in
{\lambda\mu}

[
Par92
]
.

({\textsl{Ax}})

y\mathord{:}(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}\mathrel{\vdash}{y}\colon{(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{~}

({\textsl{Ax}})

x\mathord{:}C\mathrel{\vdash}{x}\colon{C}\mathrel{\mid}{\delta\mathord{:}{\perp},\alpha\mathord{:}C,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.41878pt

(\mu)

x\mathord{:}C\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.21475pt\delta\hskip 0.21475pt{.}\hskip 0.21475pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.42952ptx}\colon{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}C,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.42952pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.88107pt})

\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda x\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026pt\mu\hskip 0.22026pt\delta\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.44054ptx}\colon{C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}C,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.44054pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})

y\mathord{:}(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}\mathrel{\vdash}{{y}\hskip 0.46342pt({\lambda x\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt\mu\hskip 0.2317pt\delta\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.46342ptx})}\colon{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}C,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.46342pt

(\mu)

y\mathord{:}(C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.23766pt\alpha\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.47531pt{y}\hskip 0.47531pt({\lambda x\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt\mu\hskip 0.23766pt\delta\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.47531ptx})}\colon{C}\mathrel{\mid}{\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.47531pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\alpha\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.4875pt{y}\hskip 0.4875pt({\lambda x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\delta\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.4875ptx})}\colon{((C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}C}\mathrel{\mid}{\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.4875pt

This corresponds to the proof in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}
above, but for the fact that extra calls to
({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.4pt})
and
({\textsl{Act}\hskip 0.4pt})
are added inside the calls to
(\mu)
, as well as additional names of type
{\perp}
; notice that because of these extra rules this term is not closed as it has a free name
\gamma
.
The proof transformation we hinted at above translates to the following (where
\Delta^{\prime}=\alpha\mathord{:}C,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp},\Delta
):

\begin{array}[]{c@{\kern-5mm}c}\begin{array}[t]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\begin{picture}(-336.0,0.0)\put(0.0,100.0){\begin{picture}\put(-336.0,160.0){\line{1}{0}{672.0}}\put(-336.0,160.0){\line{1}{-4}{35.0}}\put(336.0,160.0){\line{-1}{-4}{35.0}}\put(-306.0,20.0){\line{1}{0}{612.0}}\end{picture}}{}\put(0.0,195.0){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\mbox{$$}}}\put(0.0,-30.0){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,120.0){\hbox{${\Gamma\kern-0.85555pt,\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}\neg\neg C}$}}}\end{picture}\quad\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.8071pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=49.19713pt}

\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{PbC}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 83.47429pt\hbox{$\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}C$}}}}\end{array}\hfil\kern-14.22636pt&\begin{array}[t]{c}\\[-22.76219pt]
{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\begin{picture}(-469.0,0.0)\put(0.0,100.0){\begin{picture}\put(-469.0,160.0){\line{1}{0}{938.0}}\put(-469.0,160.0){\line{1}{-4}{35.0}}\put(469.0,160.0){\line{-1}{-4}{35.0}}\put(-439.0,20.0){\line{1}{0}{878.0}}\end{picture}}{}\put(0.0,195.0){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\mbox{$$}}}\put(0.0,-30.0){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,120.0){\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{M}\colon{(C\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\Delta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.42776pt}$}}}\end{picture}\quad\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 40.965pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=81.53352pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.79295pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma\kern-0.79295pt,y\mathord{:}C\mathrel{\vdash}{y}\colon{C}\mathrel{\mid}{\delta\mathord{:}{\perp},\Delta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.39647pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=112.51582pt}

\hbox{\kern 0.83415pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.83415pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 20.83069pt\hbox{$\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.20853pt{.}\hskip 0.20853pt{\mu\hskip 0.20853pt\delta\hskip 0.20853pt{.}\hskip 0.20853pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.41707pty}}\colon{C\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\Delta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.41707pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=254.68817pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.92685pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 76.61761pt\hbox{$\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{{M}\hskip 0.46342pt({\lambda y\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt{\mu\hskip 0.2317pt\delta\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.46342pty}})}\colon{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\Delta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.46342pt$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=285.0003pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 81.59335pt\hbox{$\begin{picture}(-694.0,0.0)\put(0.0,100.0){\begin{picture}\put(-790.0,160.0){\line{1}{-4}{35.0}}\put(790.0,160.0){\line{-1}{-4}{35.0}}\put(-760.0,20.0){\line{1}{0}{1520.0}}\end{picture}}{}\put(0.0,195.0){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,0.0){\mbox{$$}}}\put(0.0,-30.0){\pic@makebox@{\makebox}{}(0.0,120.0){\hbox{${\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.65811pt{{\lceil}\hskip 0.87749pt{{M}\hskip 0.43874pt({\lambda y\hskip 0.21936pt{.}\hskip 0.21936pt{\mu\hskip 0.21936pt\delta\hskip 0.21936pt{.}\hskip 0.21936pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.43874pty}})}\hskip 0.87749pt{\rfloor}}}\colon{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\Delta^{\prime}}\hskip 0.43874pt}$}}}\end{picture}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=295.34613pt}

so Parigot essentially replaces here an instance of the
({\textsl{Ax}})
rule for
\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\neg C\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}\neg C
by a derivation for
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-1.0pt\lambda\kern-1.0pt\mu}}{\lambda y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{\mu\hskip 0.25pt\delta\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5pty}}\colon{C\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}C,\Delta}\hskip 0.5pt
.
6
6

6

Summers
[
Sum08
]
uses
\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\hskip 0.25pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 1.5pt({zN})
.

It is this what allows for the successful encoding of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
in
{\lambda\mu}
.

We will see this kind of transformation play an important role in Section
5
.

It is important to point out that the use of
\gamma
in the previous example creates an anomaly.
Although
((C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}C
is a logical tautology, the
{\lambda\mu}
-term that is its witness is
not
a closed term so the proof has an uncanceled assumption.
Moreover, terms can have type
{\perp}
without being typed with the equivalent of rule
({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt})
, but using
({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
.

Several attempts have been made to rectify this.
Parigot not only adds
{\perp}
to the language of types (in a side remark), but also allows for statements like
\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}
to be used without adding them explicitly to the co-context, so does not consider them ‘real’ assumptions.
Ariola and Herbelin
[
AH03
]
define an extension of
{\lambda\mu}
, adding a special syntax construct
[\textrm{\sf tp}]M
, where
tp
acts as a ‘continuation constant’ and represent the outermost context of the term.
In their system, the witness to
((C\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}C
is the term
\lambda y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mu\hskip 0.25pt\alpha\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\textrm{\sf tp}]\hskip 0.5pt{y}\hskip 0.5pt({\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mu\hskip 0.25pt\delta\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5ptx})
, a closed term.

Another solution would be to detach, syntactically, passivation from activation, so to no longer insist that they strictly follow each other.
That is the approach in de Groote and Saurin’s
{\Lambda\mu}
-calculus
[
dG94
,
Sau08
]
; there the witness would be
\lambda y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{{y}\hskip 0.5pt({\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]x})}
which directly inhabits the proof in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}
above.
That variant of
{\lambda\mu}
better expresses the logic of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}
, but one problem with
{\Lambda\mu}
is that is not clear if (denotational) semantics can be defined for it, which is possible for
{\lambda\mu}

[
SR98
,
vBBd18
]
.
This is directly related to the fact that a
\mu
-abstraction can now be applied to a term of type
{\perp}
that is an application, rather than a term typed (implicitly) using rule
({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
.

2.3.
The
\nu\lambda\mu
-calculus

In
[
Sum08
]
, Summers makes a strong case for inhabiting the rules of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
directly and in full, and defines the calculus
\nu\lambda\mu
by adding the rules for negation and their syntactic representation to a generalisation of
{\lambda\mu}
.
He thereby extends the syntax with the construct
[\hskip 0.25pt{M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN
which is used to represent negation elimination, not just when
M
is a name, but also when the negated statement on the left is the result of a proof, and allows
(\mu)
to be applied to assumption used on the right in
({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
.
He also removes the distinction between names and variables, and brings all assumptions together in one context.

Definition 2.12

(Syntax of
\nu\lambda\mu
)
.

The
\nu\lambda\mu
-
terms
we consider are defined over variables (Roman characters) by the grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\quad}l}M,N&::=&x\mid\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\mid{M}\hskip 0.5ptN\mid\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\mid[\hskip 0.25pt{M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN\mid\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\end{array}

Type assignment (see Definition
2.14
below) will naturally allow
\mu
-binding to terms of the shape
[\hskip 0.25pt{P}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ
, but since
{\perp}
is a type, variables and applications can have type
{\perp}
, allowing the term
\mu\alpha.{{y}\hskip 0.5pt({\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]x})}
to be typeable; since a term like
\lambda y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP
cannot be assigned the type
{\perp}
, a term like
\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{\lambda y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP}
will not be typeable.

The reduction rules for
\nu\lambda\mu
in
[
Sum08
]
are largely defined, as can be expected, through term substitution as far as the constructors
\lambda
and
\nu
are concerned, but contracting a
\mu
redex now becomes more involved than in
{\lambda\mu}
, for the reasons we discussed in Example
1.2
.

Definition 2.13

(Reduction in
\nu\lambda\mu

[
Sum08
]
)
.

(1)

The auxiliary notion of substitution

\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{N}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
7
7

7

[
Sum08
]
uses a slightly different notation.
is defined inductively over the structure of terms, using the base cases

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\quad}l}x\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{N}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}&=&\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN\\
y\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{N}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}&=&y&(y\not=x)\\
([\hskip 0.25pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptM)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{N}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}&=&N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptM\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{N}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\end{array}

(2)

The reduction rules of
\nu\lambda\mu
are:

\begin{array}[]{rrcl}(\lambda^{\prime}):&({\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM})\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptM}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN}\\
(\nu):&[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\mu\mathord{\rightarrow}_{1}):&({\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM})\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{[\hskip 0.25pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 1.5pt({zN})}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\mu\mathord{\rightarrow}_{2}):&{N}\hskip 0.5pt({\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM})&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{[\hskip 0.25pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 1.5pt({Nz})}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{rrcl}(\mu\neg_{1}):&[\hskip 0.25pt{\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{[\hskip 0.25pt{z}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\mu\neg_{2}):&[\hskip 0.25pt{N}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5pt\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{[\hskip 0.25pt{N}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptz}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\mu\nu):&\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{z}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\mu\mu):&\mu\hskip 0.25pty\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM]&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{z}{\mathop{\cdot}}{z}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\mu\eta):&\mu\hskip 0.25ptx\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[x]\hskip 0.5ptM&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hfill(x\mathbin{\not\in}M)\end{array}

Evaluation contexts are defined by:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\hbox{{{C}}}&::=&{\lceil}\hskip 1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt{\rfloor}\mid\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{\hbox{{{C}}}}\mid{\hbox{{{C}}}}\hskip 0.5ptM\mid{M}\hskip 0.5pt{\hbox{{{C}}}}\mid\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\hbox{{{C}}}\mid[\hskip 0.25pt{\hbox{{{C}}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptM\mid[\hskip 0.25pt{M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5pt{\hbox{{{C}}}}\mid\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\hbox{{{C}}}\end{array}

(Free, unconstrained) reduction
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-0.25pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{\scriptsize$\beta\kern-0.4pt\mu$}}
on
{\mathcal{L}}
-terms is defined through

\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.75pt{{\lceil}\hskip 1.0pt{M}\hskip 1.0pt{\rfloor}}\mathrel{\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-0.5pt\mbox{{\sc n}}}}\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.75pt{{\lceil}\hskip 1.0pt{N}\hskip 1.0pt{\rfloor}}
if
M\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}N
using either of the nine rules above.

It is clear that these reduction rules contain the
cbv
-rules as well in
(\mu\mathord{\rightarrow}_{2})
and
(\mu\neg_{2})
.
Thereby reduction is not confluent; we have a critical pair in the rules
(\mu\mathord{\rightarrow}_{1})
and
(\mu\mathord{\rightarrow}_{2})
and the term
({\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM})\hskip 1.5pt({\mu\hskip 0.5pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN})
is reducible using both, and these reduction steps will (normally) result in different outcomes.

Definition 2.14

(Type assignment for
\nu\lambda\mu
)
.

(1)

The set of types is defined by the grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}A,B&::=&{\perp}\mid{\varphi}\mid A\mathop{\rightarrow}B\mid\neg A\end{array}

A
context
(of term variables)
\Gamma
is defined as before.

(2)

The type assignment rules for
\nu\lambda\mu
are:

\begin{array}[]{rl@{\dquad}rl@{\dquad}rl}({\textsl{Ax}}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.63959pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=48.79167pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,x\mathord{:}A}\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{A}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 1.0pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 4.27155pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,x\mathord{:}A}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{B}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=62.51045pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda x.M}:{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}\quad{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{A}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=87.79382pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 22.32574pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{MN}:{B}$}}}}\\[14.22636pt]
(\mu):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,x\mathord{:}\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{{\perp}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=61.71387pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 6.04906pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu x.M}:{A}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.74142pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,x\mathord{:}A}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{{\perp}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=56.8634pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\nu x.M}:{\neg A}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{\neg A}\quad{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{A}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=83.04031pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 17.4646pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{[M]N}:{{\perp}}$}}}}\end{array}

We will write
{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}{M}:{A}
for statements derivable in this system.

Notice that
{\perp}

is
a type in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
.
Because the inference rules of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
can be obtained from those of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
by removing all term information, it is immediately clear that all proofs in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
have a term representation in
\nu\lambda\mu
.

Example 2.15
.

In this calculus, the witness for double negation elimination becomes:

({\textsl{Ax}})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,x\mathord{:}\neg C}\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{\neg\neg C}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,x\mathord{:}\neg C}\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{\neg C}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,x\mathord{:}\neg C}\mathrel{\vdash}{[y]x}:{{\perp}}

(\mu)

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.23766ptx\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[y]\hskip 0.47531ptx}:{C}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

{}\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.24374ptx\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[y]\hskip 0.4875ptx}:{(\neg\neg C)\mathop{\rightarrow}C}

The presence of reduction rules
\mu\nu
and
\mu\mu
in Definition
2.13
is remarkable, since they do not correspond to proof contractions in a proof system that uses
{\perp}
only to represent conflict.
Both rule
(\mu)
and
({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt})
are only applicable to a statement of the shape
{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}{M}:{{\perp}}
; the rule
(\mu)
above them implies an assumption of the shape
\neg{\perp}
, which is allowed since in
[
Sum08
]
,
{\perp}
is a type, so the following are valid derivations.

(\mu)

{\Gamma\kern-0.95062pt,y\mathord{:}A}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{x}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766ptM}:{{\perp}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{x}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM}:{\neg A}

(\mu)

{\Gamma\kern-0.92685pt,y\mathord{:}\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.46342pt{x}\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317ptM}:{{\perp}}

(\mu)

{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{y}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt{\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{x}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766ptM}}:{A}

Moreover, treating
{\perp}
as a type gives that negation is represented in two different ways in
\nu\lambda\mu
.
In all, after the choices made by Summers, the calculus is rather too permissive and below, we will choose to not treat
{\perp}
as a type.

Example 2.16
.

We can inhabit the proof for
(A\mathop{\rightarrow}B)\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A
in both
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
and
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-1.0pt\lambda\kern-1.0pt\mu}}
.
Let
\Gamma=x\mathord{:}A\mathord{\rightarrow}B,y\mathord{:}\neg B,z\mathord{:}A
, and
\Gamma^{\prime}=x\mathord{:}A\mathord{\rightarrow}B,y\mathord{:}B\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp},z\mathord{:}A
, then we can construct, respectively:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 18.63057pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=36.8206pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.88107pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{\neg B}}$}}}}\>\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 20.92989pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=41.441pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.83755pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}}$}}}}\>\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.06876pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=27.7292pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.81662pt${\mbox{$({\textsl{Ax}})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{z}:{A}}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=131.51857pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.85904pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.3436pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 48.74364pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{xz}:{B}}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=230.0366pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.90367pt${\mbox{$({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 88.09491pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2259pt{y}\hskip 0.2259pt]\hskip 1.3555pt({xz})}:{{\perp}}}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=262.01802pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.92685pt${\mbox{$({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.37073pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 86.59032pt\hbox{${\Gamma{\setminus}z\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\hskip 0.2317pt{y}\hskip 0.2317pt]\hskip 1.39027pt({xz})}:{\neg A}}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=290.80237pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.95062pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 78.08778pt\hbox{${x\mathord{:}A\mathop{\rightarrow}B\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\hskip 0.23766pt{y}\hskip 0.23766pt]\hskip 1.42593pt({xz})}:{\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A}}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=318.48775pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 86.8939pt\hbox{${\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda xy\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\hskip 0.24374pt{y}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 1.46251pt({xz})}:{(A\mathop{\rightarrow}B)\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A}}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.89278pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=49.34502pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{y}\colon{B\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\>\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 25.10875pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=49.79872pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{x}\colon{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\>\penalty\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 18.24762pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=36.08693pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{z}\colon{A}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=107.12279pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.85904pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.3436pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 32.36688pt\hbox{$\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{xz}\colon{B}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=197.60962pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.90367pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 71.38402pt\hbox{$\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{y(xz)}\colon{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=227.92436pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.92685pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.92685pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 66.77489pt\hbox{$\Gamma^{\prime}{\setminus}z\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda z\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pty(xz)}\colon{A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=255.59816pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.95062pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 57.7146pt\hbox{$x\mathord{:}A\mathop{\rightarrow}B\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda yz\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pty(xz)}\colon{(B\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\>\penalty$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=283.28354pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 66.5512pt\hbox{$\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda xyz\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pty(xz)}\colon{(A\mathop{\rightarrow}B)\mathop{\rightarrow}(B\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{~}$}}}}\end{array}

Since this property holds in intuitionistic logic, it is no surprise that the co-context is not needed in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-1.0pt\lambda\kern-1.0pt\mu}}
.

For
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
, we can also show the counterpart,
(\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A)\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathop{\rightarrow}B
. Let
\Gamma=x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A,

y\mathord{:}A,

z\mathord{:}\neg B
.

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{z}:{\neg B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.35242pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{xz}:{\neg A}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{A}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2259pt{xz}\hskip 0.2259pt]\hskip 0.45183pty}:{{\perp}}}

(\mu)

{x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A,y\mathord{:}A\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.46342pt{z}\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt{[\hskip 0.2317pt{xz}\hskip 0.2317pt]\hskip 0.46342pty}}:{B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})

{x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{z}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt{[\hskip 0.23766pt{xz}\hskip 0.23766pt]\hskip 0.47531pty}}:{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

{\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda xy\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{z}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt{[\hskip 0.24374pt{xz}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875pty}}:{(\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A)\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}}

We can do a similar thing in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-1.0pt\lambda\kern-1.0pt\mu}}
(with
\Gamma=x\mathord{:}(B\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp},y\mathord{:}A
) :

({\textsl{Ax}})

\Gamma\kern-0.81662pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{z}\colon{B}\mathrel{\mid}{\delta\mathord{:}{\perp},\alpha\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.40831pt

(\mu)

\Gamma\kern-0.83755pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.20938pt\delta\hskip 0.20938pt{.}\hskip 0.20938pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.41878ptz}\colon{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.41878pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.85904pt})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda z\hskip 0.21475pt{.}\hskip 0.21475pt\mu\hskip 0.21475pt\delta\hskip 0.21475pt{.}\hskip 0.21475pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.42952ptz}\colon{B\mathop{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.42952pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.35242pt})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{x({\lambda z\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026pt\mu\hskip 0.22026pt\delta\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.44054ptz})}\colon{A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.44054pt

({\textsl{Ax}})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{y}\colon{A}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.42952pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{{{x}\hskip 0.45183pt({\lambda z\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259pt\mu\hskip 0.2259pt\delta\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.45183ptz})}\hskip 0.45183pty}\colon{{\perp}}\mathrel{\mid}{\alpha\mathord{:}B,\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.45183pt

(\mu)

\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.2317pt\alpha\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.46342pt{{{x}\hskip 0.46342pt({\lambda z\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt\mu\hskip 0.2317pt\delta\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.46342ptz})}\hskip 0.46342pty}}\colon{B}\mathrel{\mid}{\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.46342pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})

\Gamma{\setminus}y\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt\mu\hskip 0.23766pt\alpha\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.47531pt{{{x}\hskip 0.47531pt({\lambda z\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt\mu\hskip 0.23766pt\delta\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.47531ptz})}\hskip 0.47531pty}}\colon{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}\mathrel{\mid}{\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.47531pt

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda xy\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\alpha\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\gamma]\hskip 0.4875pt{{{x}\hskip 0.4875pt({\lambda z\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\delta\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.4875ptz})}\hskip 0.4875pty}}\colon{((B\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp})\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}\mathrel{\mid}{\gamma\mathord{:}{\perp}}\hskip 0.4875pt

Notice that the same kind of transformation has been applied to replace the negated assumption
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{z}:{\neg B}}
on the right, and that again the witness for the property is not a closed term in
{\lambda\mu}
(
\gamma
is free).

3.
The
{\mathcal{L}}
-calculus

We now present the calculus
{\mathcal{L}}
we introduce in this paper, which can be seen as a variant of
{\lambda\mu}
that gives a Curry-Howard interpretation to the logical system below, which corresponds to
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}
, extended with negation by treating it as a first-class citizen.
Our aim is to fully represent proofs in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
in a natural way, but defining a calculus with a notion of reduction that is confluent.

We call this calculus
{\mathcal{L}}
in honour of Mendelson’s formal axiomatic theory L for the propositional calculus
[
Men64
]
.
Adapted to our notation, L is defined through:

Definition 3.1

(L cf.
[
Men64
]
)
.

(1)

(Well formed) formulas are defined through the grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}A,B&::=&p\mid\neg A\mid A\mathord{\rightarrow}B\end{array}

where
p
ranges over
statement letters
.

(2)

If
A
,
B
and
C
are formulas of L, then the following are axioms of L:

A1
:

A\mathord{\rightarrow}B\mathord{\rightarrow}A
.

A2
:

(A\mathord{\rightarrow}B\mathord{\rightarrow}C)\mathord{\rightarrow}(A\mathord{\rightarrow}B)\mathord{\rightarrow}A\mathord{\rightarrow}C
.

A3
:

(\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A)\mathord{\rightarrow}(\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A)\mathord{\rightarrow}B
.

(3)

The only rule of inference of L is
modus ponens

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
:
B
is a direct consequence of
A
and
A\mathord{\rightarrow}B
.

The first two rules form the axiom-schemes for intuitionistic implicational logic; the third rule renders the system classical.
For example, using these three rules it is possible to show
\neg\neg C\mathord{\rightarrow}C
(for details, see
[
Men64
]
, Lemma 1.11(a)).

The attentive reader will recognise the types of the combinators
K
and
S
of Curry’s Combinatory Logic
[
Cur34
,
CF58
]
in the first two axioms; this is the origin of the Curry-Howard isomorphism
[
Cur34
]
.
Of course here we follow Church’s approach, by defining an extended
\lambda
-calculus.

We will base
{\mathcal{L}}
on a variant of the system
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc f}}}
defined below; notice that, because we use negation explicitly, as in
\nu\lambda\mu
we no longer have to separate the negated formulas from the non-negated ones.

\begin{array}[]{rl@{\dquad}rl@{\dquad}rl}({\textsl{Ax}}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 16.14653pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=31.80556pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,A}\mathrel{\vdash}{A}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 1.0pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.52156pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,A}\mathrel{\vdash}{B}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=32.94798pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}\quad{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{A}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=62.32162pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 20.93684pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{B}$}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rl@{\dquad}rl@{\dquad}rl@{\dquad}rl}({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,A}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=34.60971pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 3.20763pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg A}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg A}\quad{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{A}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=57.56812pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 18.8535pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{{\perp}}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\textsl{Act}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{{\perp}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=39.65137pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 9.89511pt\hbox{${\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{A}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.4pt}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.24374pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{A}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=40.13887pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{{\perp}}$}}}}\end{array}

Notice that
A
in rules
({\textsl{Act}\hskip 0.4pt})
and
({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.4pt})
can be a negated formula.
The rule
({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.4pt})
could be omitted, since, as before, we can derive:

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.95062pt,\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg A}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{{\perp}}

We keep the rule, however, since we want to preserve the fact that in rule
({\textsl{Act}\hskip 0.4pt})
we only cancel a negated assumption that was used on the left in
({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
; notice that that characteristic is not expressed in the logic, but will be once we represent the structure of proofs through syntax.

Definition 3.2

(Syntax of
{\mathcal{L}}
)
.

The set of
{\mathcal{L}}
-
terms
we consider is defined over variables and names by the grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\quad}l}M,N&::=&x\mid\lambda x.M\mid MN\mid\nu x.M\mid[M]N\mid\mu\alpha.M\mid[\alpha]N\end{array}

Notice that
\alpha
is not a term.
Since
{\perp}
is not a type, type assignment (see Definition
3.7
below) will only allow
\mu
-binding to terms of the shape
[\alpha]Q
or
[\hskip 0.25pt{P}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ
, so staying close to
{\lambda\mu}
.

We will use
{\mathcal{L}}
for the set of terms defined above, as well as for the system based on that, including the reduction and type assignment rules.
In
{\mathcal{L}}
, reduction of terms is expressed via three types of implicit substitution.
As usual,
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
stands for the (instantaneous) substitution of all occurrences of
x
in
M
by
N
.
The definition of structural substitution for
{\mathcal{L}}
is defined as for
{\lambda\mu}
(Definition
2.5
), but with small modifications.

Definition 3.3

(Structural substitution in
{\mathcal{L}}
)
.

Structural substitution
,
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
and
insertion

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
are defined inductively over terms.
We give the main cases:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}([\alpha]M)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}&\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}&[\gamma](M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}N)\\
{}([\beta]M)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}&\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}&[\beta](M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\})~(\beta\not=\alpha)\\
\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{rcl}([\alpha]M)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}&\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}&[\hskip 0.25pt{M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN\\
{}([\beta]M)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}&\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}&[\beta](M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\})~(\beta\not=\alpha)\\
\end{array}

We have the following notion of reduction on
{\mathcal{L}}
.

Definition 3.4

(
{\mathcal{L}}
reduction)
.

(1)

The reduction rules of
{\mathcal{L}}
are:

\begin{array}[]{rrcl}(\beta):&({\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM})\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\nu):&[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\mu):&({\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM})\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\gamma}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\quad(\gamma\textit{ fresh})\\
\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{rrcl}(\delta):&[\hskip 0.25pt{\mu\alpha.M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\\
(\theta):&\mu\hskip 0.25pt\alpha\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5ptM&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\quad(\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}M)\\
(\rho):&[\hskip 0.25pt{\beta}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5pt\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\gamma}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\gamma\hskip-0.5pt\}\end{array}

Evaluation contexts are defined by:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\hbox{{{C}}}&::=&{\lceil}\hskip 1.0pt\hskip 1.0pt{\rfloor}\mid\lambda x.{\hbox{{{C}}}}\mid{\hbox{{{C}}}}M\mid M{\hbox{{{C}}}}\mid\nu x.\hbox{{{C}}}\mid[\hbox{{{C}}}]M\mid[M]{\hbox{{{C}}}}\mid\mu\alpha.\hbox{{{C}}}\mid[\alpha]{\hbox{{{C}}}}\end{array}

Reduction
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
on
{\mathcal{L}}
-terms is defined through

\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.75pt{{\lceil}\hskip 1.0pt{M}\hskip 1.0pt{\rfloor}}\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.75pt{{\lceil}\hskip 1.0pt{N}\hskip 1.0pt{\rfloor}}
if
M\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}N
using either the
\beta
,
\nu
,
\mu
,
\delta
,
\theta
, or
\rho
-reduction rule.
As usual, we will use
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}^{=}}
for the reflexive closure, and
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
for the reflexive, transitive closure of
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.

Since syntax and reduction rules for
{\mathcal{L}}
are direct extensions of those for
{\lambda\mu}
, we can show easily that reduction in
{\mathcal{L}}
is a conservative extension of reduction in
{\lambda\mu}
.

Theorem 3.5
.

If
M
and
N
are
{\lambda\mu}
terms such that
M\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\hbox{\raise 4.5pt\hbox{\scriptsize${\ast}$}\kern-4.09723pt\hbox{${\kern-0.25pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{\scriptsize$\beta\kern-0.4pt\mu$}}$}}}N
, then
M\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N
.

Proof 3.6
.

Straightforward. ∎

A similar result cannot be shown for
\nu\lambda\mu
, nor for
cbv
reduction in
{\lambda\mu}
.

Type assignment for
{\mathcal{L}}
is defined through:

Definition 3.7

(Type assignment for
{\mathcal{L}}
)
.

(1)

The set of types
{{\mathcal{T}}_{\kern-1.0pt{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}
is defined by the grammar:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}A,B&::=&{\varphi}\mid A\mathop{\rightarrow}B\mid\neg A\end{array}

where ‘
\mathord{\rightarrow}
’ associates to the right and ‘
\neg
’ binds stronger than ‘
\mathord{\rightarrow}
’.
If
A=\neg B
, we call
A
a
negated
type, and if
A=\neg B
, but
B\not=\neg C
, we call
A
a
single negated
type.
If
A=\neg\neg B
, we call
A
a
double negated type
, where
B
could be a negated type as well.

(2)

A
context

\Gamma
is defined as a partial mapping from term variables to types (which can be negated) and names to negated types, denoted as a finite set of
statements

x\mathord{:}A
and
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg B
, such that the
subjects
of the statements are distinct.

We define
\overline{\Gamma}
through:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\overline{\varnothing}&=&\varnothing\\
\overline{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}A}&=&\overline{\Gamma},A\\
\overline{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A}&=&\overline{\Gamma},\neg A\end{array}

(3)

The type assignment rules for
{\mathcal{L}}
are:

\begin{array}[]{c}\begin{array}[]{rl@{\quad}rl@{\quad}rl}({\textsl{Ax}})\mathord{:}&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.63959pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=48.79167pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,x\mathord{:}A\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{A}}$}}}}&(\mu):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{{\perp}}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=62.39561pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 6.04906pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\alpha.M}:{A}}$}}}}&(\textsl{N})\mathord{:}&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 20.9463pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{A}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=73.169pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]N}:{{\perp}}}$}}}}\end{array}\\[14.22636pt]
\begin{array}[]{rl@{\quad}rl}({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 1.0pt})\mathord{:}&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 4.27155pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,x\mathord{:}A\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{B}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=62.51045pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda x.M}:{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}}$}}}}&({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})\mathord{:}&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}}\quad{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{A}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=87.79382pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 22.32574pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{MN}:{B}}$}}}}\end{array}\\[14.22636pt]
\begin{array}[]{rl@{\quad}rl}({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt})\mathord{:}&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.74142pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,x\mathord{:}A\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{{\perp}}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=56.8634pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\nu x.M}:{\neg A}}$}}}}&({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})\mathord{:}&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{\neg A}}\quad{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{A}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=83.04031pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 17.4646pt\hbox{${\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[M]N}:{{\perp}}}$}}}}\end{array}\end{array}

We will write
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
for statements derivable in this system.

Notice that
{\perp}
is not a type.
Also, in rule
(\textsl{N})
,
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A
is added to the context; this allows for that statement to already occur there.
In all the rules where a variable or name is bound, by our variable convention it does not occur in the context in the conclusion.
The notation
\overline{\Gamma}
will be used in Corollary
5.4
.

Example 3.8
.

In this calculus,
\lambda y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 1.5pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]x})}
is the witness for double negation elimination:

({\textsl{Ax}})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{\neg\neg C}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,x\mathord{:}C\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{C}}

(\textsl{N})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,x\mathord{:}C,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]x}:{{\perp}}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.35242pt})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026pt[\alpha]x}:{\neg C}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2317pt{y}\hskip 0.2317pt]\hskip 1.39027pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\alpha]x})}:{{\perp}}}

(\mu)

{y\mathord{:}\neg\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt{[\hskip 0.23766pt{y}\hskip 0.23766pt]\hskip 1.42593pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\alpha]x})}}:{C}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

{\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt{[\hskip 0.24374pt{y}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 1.46251pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]x})}}:{(\neg\neg C)\mathop{\rightarrow}C}}

Observe that
\lambda y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 1.5pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]x})}
is a closed term.

It is also straightforward to find untypeable terms. For example, we cannot type a term like
[\hskip 0.25pt{\lambda x.M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN
since type assignment would require a negated type for
\lambda x.M
, nor
\mu\alpha.\lambda x.M
since that would require
{\perp}
for
\lambda x.M
, nor
\nu y.\lambda x.M
,
\mu\alpha.MN
,
\mu\alpha.\nu x.M
, etc.

We can also how that type assignment in
{\mathcal{L}}
is a conservative extension of that in
{\lambda\mu}
.

Theorem 3.9
.

If
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-1.0pt\lambda\kern-1.0pt\mu}}{M}\colon{A}\mathrel{\mid}{\Delta}\hskip 0.5pt
, then
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\neg\Delta\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
.

Proof 3.10
.

Easy. ∎

Again, a similar result cannot be shown for
\nu\lambda\mu
.

It will be clear that, once allowing Greek characters for variables as well, the rule
(\textsl{N})
is admissible in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
, as was the case above for rule
({\textsl{Pass}\hskip 0.4pt})
.
Observe that, if
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{{\perp}}}
, in order for the derivation for
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{\mu\alpha.M}:{A}}
to be used as a subderivation, either
A=B\mathord{\rightarrow}C
, or
A=\neg B
, for some
B
and
C
.

We will now show that types are preserved under reduction.
For this we need a weakening result.

Lemma 3.11

(Weakening and thinning for
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
)
.

The following rules are admissible for
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
:

\begin{array}[]{rl@{\dquad}rl}(\textsl{Wk}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.64653pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{A}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=36.23615pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$(\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{A}}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&(\textsl{Th}):&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.64653pt\hbox{$\penalty{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{A}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=36.23615pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$(\Gamma^{\prime}=\{\,\hbox{$x\mathord{:}B\mathbin{\in}\Gamma\mid x\mathbin{\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.24374pt({M})$}\,\})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\vdash}{M}:{A}}$}}}}\end{array}

Proof 3.12
.

Standard.∎

Notice that, by our extension of Barendregt’s convention in Definition
2.7
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
cannot contain statements for the bound names and variables in
M
.

Example 3.13
.

We illustrate the reduction rule
\delta
:

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.35242pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026ptP}:{\neg A}}

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.90367pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2259pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.2259pt]\hskip 0.45183pt\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259ptP}:{{\perp}}}

(\mu)

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\alpha.M}:{\neg A}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.24374pt{\mu\alpha.M}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875ptN}:{{\perp}}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.36145pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259ptP}:{\neg A}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2317pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317ptP}\hskip 0.2317pt]\hskip 0.46342ptN}:{{\perp}}}

It might have been more natural, similar to the approach of
[
Sum08
]
, to define

\begin{array}[]{rcl}([\alpha]M)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}&\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}&[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[z]N}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptM\end{array}

which would have created the subterm
[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[z]N}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptP
in the derivation above; however, notice that
[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[z]N}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptP\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}[\hskip 0.25pt{P}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN
and that
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[z]N{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
only ever gets applied ‘to the left’.

We will now show that type assignment is closed under reduction.
First we show results for the three notions of term substitution.

Lemma 3.14

(Substitution lemma)
.

(1)

If
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}B\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{L}:{B}}
, then

{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptL{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{A}}
.

(2)

If
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg(B\mathop{\rightarrow}C)\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{L}:{B}}
, then
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\gamma\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{L}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{A}}
.

(3)

If
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg\neg B\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{L}:{B}}
, then
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{A}}
.

Proof 3.15
.

(1)

Standard, by induction on the definition of term substitution.

(2)

By induction on the definition of structural substitution.
All cases follow straightforwardly, except for:

([\alpha]N)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{L}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}[\hskip 0.25pt{\gamma}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{L}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
:

Then we have
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg(B\mathop{\rightarrow}C)\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{N}:{B\mathop{\rightarrow}C}}
by rule
(\textsl{N})
, and
A={\perp}
.
Then, by induction, we have

{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\gamma\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{L}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{B\mathop{\rightarrow}C}}
.
Since we know that
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{L}:{B}}
, we can construct:

(\textsl{Wk})

{\Gamma\kern-0.85555pt,\gamma\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{L}:{B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38023pt})

{\Gamma\kern-0.95062pt,\gamma\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{({N\hskip 0.71297pt\{\hskip-0.47531pt{L}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.47531pt\}})\hskip 0.47531ptL}:{C}}

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\gamma\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{[\gamma]({N\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{L}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}})\hskip 0.4875ptL}:{{\perp}}}

(3)

By induction on the definition of insertion.
All cases follow straightforwardly, except for:

([\alpha]M)\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}[\hskip 0.25pt{M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN
:

Then
A={\perp}
, and the derivation is of the shape:

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]P}:{{\perp}}}

By induction we have
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{\neg B}}
, and we can construct:

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{[{P\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}}]}}N:{\perp}

∎

Notice that the structural substitution
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
gets performed by building an application with any subterm
P
that is named
\alpha
, resulting in
[\gamma]PN
of type
B
.
Moreover, the insertion
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
gets performed for typed terms towards a name that has a double negated type, which disappears.

We will now show that type assignment respects reduction:

Theorem 3.16

(Soundness)
.

If
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
, and
M\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N
, then
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{N}:{A}}
.

Proof 3.17
.

By induction on the definition of
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
, where we focus on the basic reduction rules.

(\beta)
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}({\lambda x.P})\hskip 0.5ptQ\mathrel{\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-0.5pt\mbox{{\sc n}}}}P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}N
.
The derivation for
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{({\lambda x.P})\hskip 0.5ptQ}:{A}}
is shaped like

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda x.P}:{B\mathop{\rightarrow}A}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{({\lambda x.P})\hskip 0.4875ptQ}:{A}}

In particular, we have
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}B\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P}:{A}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{Q}:{B}}
.
Then we have
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{A}}
by Lemma
3.14
.

(\nu)
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ\mathrel{\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-0.5pt\mbox{{\sc n}}}}P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}N
.
Then
A={\perp}
and the derivation for
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ}:{{\perp}}}
is shaped like

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.38023pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\nu x.P}:{\neg B}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.24374pt{\nu x.P}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875ptQ}:{{\perp}}}

In particular, we have
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}B\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P}:{{\perp}}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{Q}:{B}}
.
Then we have
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{{\perp}}}
by Lemma
3.14
.

(\mu)
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}({\mu\alpha.P})\hskip 0.5ptQ\mathrel{\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-0.5pt\mbox{{\sc n}}}}\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\gamma}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{Q}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}N
.
The derivation for
({\mu\alpha.P})\hskip 0.5ptQ
is shaped like

(\mu)

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\alpha.P}:{B\mathop{\rightarrow}A}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{({\mu\alpha.P})\hskip 0.4875ptQ}:{A}}

In particular, we have
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg(B\mathop{\rightarrow}A)\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P}:{{\perp}}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{Q}:{B}}
.
Then by Lemma
3.14
, we have
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\gamma\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{Q}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{{\perp}}}
, and applying rule
(\mu)
gives the result.

(\delta)
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}[\hskip 0.25pt{\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ\mathrel{\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-0.5pt\mbox{{\sc n}}}}P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}N
.
Then
A={\perp}
and the derivation for
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{[\hskip 0.25pt{\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ}:{{\perp}}}
is shaped like

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.38023pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\alpha.P}:{\neg B}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.24374pt{\mu\alpha.P}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875ptQ}:{{\perp}}}

In particular, we have
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg\neg B\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P}:{{\perp}}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{Q}:{B}}
.
Then, by Lemma
3.14
, we have
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{{\perp}}}
.

(\theta)
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\hskip 0.25pt\alpha\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5ptP\mathrel{\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-0.5pt\mbox{{\sc n}}}}P\mathrel{\equiv}N
with
\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}M
.
The derivation for
\mu\hskip 0.25pt\alpha\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5ptP
is shaped like

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.95062pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]P}:{{\perp}}}

(\mu)

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.24374pt\alpha\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.4875ptP}:{A}}

We have
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P}:{A}}
through a sub-derivation.

(\rho)
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}[\beta]\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\gamma}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\mathrel{\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-0.5pt\mbox{{\sc n}}}}M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\gamma\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}N
.
The derivation for
[\beta]\mu\gamma.P
is shaped like

(\mu)

{\Gamma\kern-0.95062pt,\beta\mathord{:}\neg B\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\gamma.P}:{B}}

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\beta\mathord{:}\neg B\mathrel{\vdash}{[\beta]\mu\gamma.P}:{{\perp}}}

So in particular, replacing all
\gamma
by
\beta
, we obtain
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\beta\mathord{:}\neg B\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\gamma\hskip-0.5pt\}}:{{\perp}}}
.
∎

4.
Confluence

In this section we will show that reduction in
{\mathcal{L}}
satisfies the Church-Rosser property,
i.e.
is confluent.
This property is defined as follows:

Definition 4.1

(Diamond and Church-Rosser Properties
[
Bar84
]
)
.

Let
\mathbin{R}
be binary relation on a set
V
.

(1)

\mathbin{R}
satisfies the
diamond property
if for all
t,u,v\mathbin{\in}V
, if
t\mathbin{R}u
and
t\mathbin{R}v
, then there exists
w\mathbin{\in}V
such that
u\mathbin{R}w
and
v\mathbin{R}w
.

(2)

\mathbin{R}
satisfies the
Church-Rosser property
(is
confluent
) if its reflexive, transitive closure
\mathbin{R}^{*}
satisfies the diamond property.

This immediately implies that if a relation is confluent, then so is its transitive closure.

The standard approach to showing confluence is that of Tait and Martin-Löf (see
[
Bar84
,
Pfe92
]
) by defining a notion of
parallel reduction
that is based on the standard reduction, which is a reflexive relation defined (in the case of
\beta
-reduction) through the rules:

\begin{array}[]{c@{\dquad}c@{\dquad}c@{\dquad}c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 10.95901pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=21.43053pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$x\mathbin{\Rightarrow}x$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 15.05766pt\hbox{$\penalty M\mathbin{\Rightarrow}M^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=64.01671pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\lambda x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\lambda x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty M\mathbin{\Rightarrow}M^{\prime}\quad N\mathbin{\Rightarrow}N^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=74.95691pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 9.26877pt\hbox{${M}\hskip 0.4875ptN\mathbin{\Rightarrow}{M}\hskip 0.4875pt^{\prime}N^{\prime}$}}}}\hfil\quad\quad&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 12.60869pt\hbox{$\penalty M\mathbin{\Rightarrow}M^{\prime}\quad N\mathbin{\Rightarrow}N^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=100.17429pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$({\lambda x.M})\hskip 0.4875ptN\mathbin{\Rightarrow}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}\end{array}

By the last rule, ‘
\mathbin{\Rightarrow}
’ encompasses ‘
\mathord{\rightarrow}_{\kern-1.0pt\beta}
’; also, if
N
reduces to
N^{\prime}
, then
({\lambda x.M})\hskip 0.5ptN
reduces to
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
, so all contractions in the various copies of
N
inside
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
are contracted simultaneously when contracting the redex
({\lambda x.M})\hskip 0.5ptN
; we are even allowed to contract a redex in
M
, and contracting all these together is considered a
single step
in ‘
\mathbin{\Rightarrow}
’.
The proof of confluence for
\beta
-reduction then contains of showing that
\mathbin{\Rightarrow}
satisfies the diamond property, and that
{\mathbin{\Rightarrow}}={\mathrel{{\mathord{\rightarrow}\hbox{\raise 4.5pt\hbox{\scriptsize$\ast$}\kern-4.09723pt}_{\kern-1.0pt\beta}}\kern 0.0pt}}
.

Using this technique, confluence has been claimed for
{\lambda\mu}
in
[
Par92
]
, but, as noticed in
[
Py98
,
BHF01
]
, that proof was not complete.
The main reason is that the proof overlooks the fact that, perhaps unexpectedly, contraction of one redex can remove another.

Example 4.2
.

Take the term
({\mu\alpha.[\alpha]\mu\beta.[\alpha]M})\hskip 0.5ptN
; observe that contracting the outermost
\mu
-redex
({\mu\alpha.\ldots})\hskip 0.5ptN
destroys the innermost
\rho
-redex
\mu\alpha.[\alpha]\mu\beta.[\alpha]M
.
The latter is contractable because the sub-term
\mu\beta.[\alpha]M
is a
\mu
-abstraction:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}({\mu\alpha.[\alpha]\mu\beta.[\alpha]M})\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathbin{\Rightarrow}(\rho)&({\mu\hskip 0.25pt\alpha\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5ptM})\hskip 0.5ptN\end{array}

This is no longer true after the contraction of the outermost redex:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}({\mu\alpha.[\alpha]\mu\beta.[\alpha]M})\hskip 0.5ptN&\mathbin{\Rightarrow}(\mu)&\mu\gamma.[\gamma]({\mu\beta.[\gamma]MN})\hskip 0.5ptN\end{array}

where
N
gets (also) placed as an argument to
\mu\beta.[\gamma]MN
, creating the application
({\mu\beta.[\gamma]MN})\hskip 0.5ptN
which means that the result is no longer a
\mu
-abstraction, thus destroying the
\rho
-redex.
The resulting terms can be joined, but not through a single parallel reduction step, as would be required.
So the diamond property does not hold for the standard notion of parallel reduction.

This problem was successfully addressed by Py
[
Py98
]
and later in
[
BHF01
]
using a slightly different approach.
We will follow the solution of the first here, using the modification of the definition of ‘
\mathbin{\Rightarrow}
’ as suggested by Aczel
[
Acz78
]
.

As in
[
BHF01
,
vB18
,
vBBd18
]
,
we will not consider the extensional erasure reduction rule

\begin{array}[]{rrcl@{\quad}l}\theta:&\mu\hskip 0.25pt\alpha\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]\hskip 0.5ptM&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}}&M&(\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}M)\\
\end{array}

Below we will need the property that we can change the order in which the four implicit substitution operations are performed.
Notice that we can consider the substitution a binding operation for the variable or name involved, so for example the variable
x
in
