M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}
can be assumed to not occur in
P
.

Proposition 4.3
.

(1)

(a)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(b)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(c)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(d)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(2)

(a)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(b)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(c)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(d)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(3)

(a)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(b)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(c)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(d)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(4)

(a)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(b)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{P}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(c)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(d)

M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\delta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

Proof 4.4
.

Straightforward by induction on the definition of the four substitutions.
∎

We now define a notion of parallel reduction for
{\mathcal{L}}
.

Definition 4.5

(Generalised Parallel Reduction for
{\mathcal{L}}
(cf.
[
Acz78
,
Py98
]
))
.

We define
parallel reduction
on terms in
{\mathcal{L}}
inductively by the rules:

\begin{array}[]{rl}(1)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.73672pt\hbox{$\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=26.98595pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$x\Rightarrow x$}}}}\\
(2)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 15.05766pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow M^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=69.57213pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\lambda x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM\Rightarrow\lambda x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}$}}}}\\
(3)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 17.11067pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow M^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=73.67815pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\mu\alpha.M\Rightarrow\mu\alpha.M^{\prime}$}}}}\\
(4)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 13.24675pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow M^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=65.9503pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM\Rightarrow\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}$}}}}\\
\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{rl}(5)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\quad N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=86.06775pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 13.43533pt\hbox{${M}\hskip 0.4875ptN\Rightarrow{M}\hskip 0.4875pt^{\prime}N^{\prime}$}}}}\\
(6)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\quad N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=86.06775pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 6.97841pt\hbox{$[M]N\Rightarrow[M^{\prime}]N^{\prime}$}}}}\\
(7)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 12.19635pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow M^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=63.8495pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$[\alpha]M\Rightarrow[\alpha]M^{\prime}$}}}}\\
(8)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 11.1668pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=77.96567pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$[\beta]M\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}\end{array}\quad\begin{array}[]{rl}(9)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow\lambda x.M^{\prime}\quad N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=102.06079pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 10.05096pt\hbox{${M}\hskip 0.4875ptN\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}\\
(10)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.65413pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}\quad N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=105.55128pt}
\hbox{\kern 0.975pt${\mbox{$(\gamma\textit{ fresh})$}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{${M}\hskip 0.4875ptN\Rightarrow\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{\gamma}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{N^{\prime}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}\\
(11)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}\quad N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=97.68188pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 4.83998pt\hbox{$[\hskip 0.24374pt{M}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875ptN\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}\\
(12)&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty M\Rightarrow\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptM^{\prime}\quad N\Rightarrow N^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=102.24303pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 8.66995pt\hbox{$[M]N\Rightarrow M\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}\end{array}

We write
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N
if the statement
M\Rightarrow N
is derivable using these rules.

It is easy to check that a term parallel reduces to itself, and under parallel reduction a term is considered to be in normal form if it only reduces to itself.

Notice, in particular, the change in the rule based on
\beta
-reduction, which changes from

\begin{array}[]{c@{\quad\textrm{ to }\quad}c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 12.60869pt\hbox{$\penalty M\mathbin{\Rightarrow}M^{\prime}\quad N\mathbin{\Rightarrow}N^{\prime}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=100.17429pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$({\lambda x.M})\hskip 0.4875ptN\mathbin{\Rightarrow}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}&{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\penalty M\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,\lambda x.M^{\prime}\quad N\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,N^{\prime}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt{\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=97.61646pt}
\hbox{}}}\hbox{\kern 8.93988pt\hbox{${M}\hskip 0.4875ptN\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.4875pt\}$}}}}\end{array}

It is this change that solves the problem mentioned.

Example 4.6
.

The problem signalled in Example
4.2
does not occur, since the diverging reduction steps

(10)

({\mu\alpha.[\alpha]\mu\beta.P})\hskip 0.4875ptN\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,\mu\gamma.[\gamma](\mu\beta.P\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\})N

(7)

[\alpha]\mu\beta.P\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,P\hskip 0.67775pt\{\hskip-0.45183pt\alpha{/}\beta\hskip-0.45183pt\}

(3)

\mu\alpha.[\alpha]\mu\beta.P\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,\mu\alpha.P\hskip 0.69513pt\{\hskip-0.46342pt\alpha{/}\beta\hskip-0.46342pt\}

(5)

({\mu\alpha.[\alpha]\mu\beta.P})\hskip 0.47531ptN\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,({\mu\alpha.P\hskip 0.71297pt\{\hskip-0.47531pt\alpha{/}\beta\hskip-0.47531pt\}})\hskip 0.47531ptN

can be joined:

(10)

(\mu\beta.P\hskip 0.69513pt\{\hskip-0.46342pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.46342pt\})N\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,\mu\delta.P\hskip 0.69513pt\{\hskip-0.46342pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.46342pt\}\hskip 0.69513pt\{\hskip-0.46342pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\beta\hskip-0.46342pt\}

(7)

[\gamma](\mu\beta.P\hskip 0.71297pt\{\hskip-0.47531pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.47531pt\})N\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,P\hskip 0.71297pt\{\hskip-0.47531pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.47531pt\}\hskip 0.71297pt\{\hskip-0.47531pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\beta\hskip-0.47531pt\}\hskip 0.71297pt\{\hskip-0.47531pt\gamma{/}\delta\hskip-0.47531pt\}

(3)

\mu\gamma.[\gamma](\mu\beta.P\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\})N\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,\mu\gamma.P\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\beta\hskip-0.4875pt\}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt\gamma{/}\delta\hskip-0.4875pt\}

(10)

({\mu\alpha.P\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt\alpha{/}\beta\hskip-0.4875pt\}})\hskip 0.4875ptN\,\mathbin{\Rightarrow}\,\mu\gamma.P\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt\alpha{/}\beta\hskip-0.4875pt\}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}

(notice that
\mu\gamma.P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\alpha{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=\mu\gamma.P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\delta}{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\gamma{/}\delta\hskip-0.5pt\}
).

It is straightforward to show that
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is the transitive closure of
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.

Lemma 4.7
.

{\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}={\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}^{*}}}
.

Proof 4.8
.

First, since
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}M
, for all
M
, by the presence of rules
(9)
,
(10)
,
(11)
, and
(12)
, we have
{\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}^{=}}}\subseteq{\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}
, so also
{\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}\subseteq{\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}^{*}}}
.
Since in
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
we essentially contract any number of
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
-redexes in parallel (including zero or just one) we also have that
{\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}\subseteq{\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}
.
So in particular,
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is a subset of a relation that is transitive, so its transitive closure is that as well, so
{\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}^{*}}}\subseteq{\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}
.
So
{\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}}={\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}^{*}}}
.
∎

The following property expresses that the four kinds of substitution are respected by
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.

Lemma 4.9

(Substitution Lemma)
.

If
P\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P^{\prime}
and
Q\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q^{\prime}
, then:

(1)
P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
,
(2)
P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{Q}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{Q^{\prime}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
,
(3)
P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
, and
(4)
P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

Proof 4.10
.

(1)

By induction on the definition of
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
, where we focus on the first parallel reduction.

(1)
:

\begin{array}[t]{lll}P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}=z\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}=Q\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q^{\prime}=z\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}=P^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\},\textrm{ and}\\
P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}=y\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}=y\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}y=y\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}=P^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\textrm{ if }y\not=z.\end{array}

(9)
:

Then
MN\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows from
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\lambda x.M^{\prime}
and
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}
. By induction, we have
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\lambda x.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
and
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
So we can infer:

\penalty M\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\lambda x.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\quad\quad N\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}

(9)

{M}\hskip 0.4875pt\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}N\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}{/}x\hskip-0.4875pt\}

By Lemma
4.3
, we have
M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}=M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(10)
:

Then
MN\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mu\gamma.M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N^{\prime}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows from
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mu\alpha.M^{\prime}
and
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}
.
By induction,
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\mu\alpha.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
and
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
So we can infer:

\penalty M\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\mu\alpha.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\quad\quad N\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}

(10)

{M}\hskip 0.4875pt\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}N\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mu\gamma.M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875pt{N^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}

We have
\mu\gamma.M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=\mu\gamma.M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N^{\prime}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
by Lemma
4.3
.

(11)
:

Then
[M]N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows from
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\nu x.M^{\prime}
and
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}
.
By induction,
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\nu x.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
and
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
So we can infer:

\penalty M\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\nu x.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\quad\quad N\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}

(9)

[\hskip 0.24374pt{M\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875ptN\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}{/}x\hskip-0.4875pt\}

By Lemma
4.3
, we have
M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}=M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(12)
:

Then
[M]N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows from
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mu\alpha.M^{\prime}
and
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}
.
By induction,
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\mu\alpha.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
and
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
So we can infer:

\penalty M\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}(\mu\alpha.M^{\prime})\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\quad\quad N\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}

(12)

[\hskip 0.24374pt{M\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875ptN\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.46251pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}M^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptN^{\prime}\hskip 0.73125pt\{\hskip-0.4875ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.4875pt\}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.4875pt\}

By Lemma
4.3
, we have
M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}=M^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN^{\prime}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ^{\prime}{/}z\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

The other cases all follow by induction.

(2)

,
(\textit{3})
and
(\textit{4})
Very similar. ∎

The following property expresses the interaction between the syntactic structure of terms and
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.

Proposition 4.11
.

(1)

If
\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}L
, then
L\mathrel{\equiv}\lambda x.N
and
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N
.

(2)

If
\mu\alpha.M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}L
, then
L\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN
and
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N
.

(3)

If
\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}L
, then
L\mathrel{\equiv}\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN
and
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}N
.

(4)

If
{M}\hskip 0.5ptN\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}L
, then either:

(a)

L\mathrel{\equiv}{P}\hskip 0.5ptQ
with
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P
and
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q
, or

(b)

M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\lambda x.P
, and
L=P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q
, or

(c)

M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mu\alpha.P
, and
L=\mu\gamma.P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{Q}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q
.

(5)

If
[\hskip 0.25pt{M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}L
, then either:

(a)

L\mathrel{\equiv}[\hskip 0.25pt{P}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ
with
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P
and
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q
, or

(b)

M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP
, and
L=P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q
, or

(c)

M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mu\alpha.P
, and
L=P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
N\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}Q
.

(6)

If
[\alpha]M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}L
, then either:

(a)

L\mathrel{\equiv}[\alpha]P
with
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P
, or

(b)

L\mathrel{\equiv}P\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\alpha{/}\beta\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\beta}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP
.

Proof 4.12
.

Straightforward by the definition of
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.
∎

We now show that
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
satisfies the diamond property.
We will write ‘
P_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}P_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}P_{2}
’ for ‘
P_{1}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P_{3}
and
P_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P_{3}
’.

Theorem 4.13
.

If
P_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P_{1}
and
P_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}P_{2}
then there exists a
P_{3}
such that
P_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}P_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}P_{2}
.

Proof 4.14
.

By induction on the definition of
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}
, where we focus on the first parallel reduction.
We only show the interesting cases.

(1)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}x\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}x\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
, and
P_{2}=x
; take
P_{3}=x
as well.

(2),(3),(4)
:

By induction.

(5)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}{M}\hskip 0.5pt_{0}N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}{M}\hskip 0.5pt_{1}N_{1}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{1}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
4
), either:

P_{2}=M_{2}N_{2}
, with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
.
Take
P_{3}={M}\hskip 0.5pt_{3}N_{3}
.

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\lambda x.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\lambda x.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; by Proposition
4.11
(
1
),
M_{3}=\lambda x.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}
.
By Rule 9, we have
{M}\hskip 0.5pt_{1}N_{1}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
, and by Lemma
4.9
, we have
M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\gamma.M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}.\gamma{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists

M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; by Proposition
4.11
(
2
),
M_{3}=\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}
.
By Rule 10, we have
{M}\hskip 0.5pt_{1}N_{1}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\gamma.M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{3}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
, and by Lemma
4.9
, we have
\mu\gamma.M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{2}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\gamma.M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{3}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(6)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}[\hskip 0.25pt{M_{0}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}[\hskip 0.25pt{M_{1}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN_{1}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{1}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
5
), either:

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}[M_{2}]N_{2}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
.
Take
P_{3}=[\hskip 0.25pt{M_{3}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN_{3}
.

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\nu x.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\nu x.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; by Proposition
4.11
(
1
),
M_{3}=\nu x.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}
.
By Rule (11), we have
[\hskip 0.25pt{M_{1}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN_{1}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
and by Lemma
4.9
, we have
M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

P_{2}=M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; by Proposition
4.11
(
2
),
M_{3}=\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}
.
By Rule (12), we have
[\hskip 0.25pt{M_{1}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN_{1}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
By Lemma
4.9
, we have
M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{3}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(7)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}[\beta]M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}[\beta]M_{1}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
6
), either:

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}[\beta]M_{2}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
:

By induction, there exists
M_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
; then by Rule
(7)
also

[\beta]M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}[\beta]M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}[\beta]M_{2}
.

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM_{2}
:

By induction, there exists
M_{3}
such that
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}

M_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
2
),
M_{3}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
,
M_{1}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{1}^{\prime}
. By Lemma
4.9
we have that
M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
Since
\mu\alpha.M_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
, by Rule
(7)
also

[\beta]\mu\alpha.M_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(8)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}[\beta]M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
6
), either:

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}[\beta]M_{2}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
:

By induction, there exists
M_{3}
such that
\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}

\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
2
),
M_{3}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
M_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{2}^{\prime}
. By Lemma
4.9
we have that
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
Since
\mu\alpha.M_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
, by Rule
(7)
also

[\beta]\mu\alpha.M_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM_{2}
:

By induction, there exists
M_{3}
such that
\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}

\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
2
),
M_{3}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
.
Then by Lemma
4.9
, also
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\beta{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

(9)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}{M}\hskip 0.5pt_{0}N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\lambda x.M_{1}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
4
), either:

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}M_{2}N_{2}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
\lambda x.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
1
),
M_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}\lambda x.M_{2}^{\prime}
and
M_{3}\mathrel{\equiv}\lambda x.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
.
Since
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\lambda x.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{3}
, by Rule
(9)
,
M_{2}N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
We have
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
by Lemma
4.9
.

P_{2}=M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\lambda x.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
\lambda x.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\lambda x.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
1
),
M_{3}=\lambda x.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
.
Then
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows by Lemma
4.9
.

P_{2}=\mu\gamma.M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{2}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists

M_{3}
such that
\lambda x.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
; this is impossible.

(10)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}{M}\hskip 0.5pt_{0}N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\gamma.M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{1}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{1}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
4
), either:

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}M_{2}N_{2}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exist
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that

\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
2
),
M_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{2}^{\prime}
and
M_{3}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
.
Since
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{3}
, by Rule
(10)
,
M_{2}N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\gamma.M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{3}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
, and
\mu\gamma.M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{1}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\gamma.M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{3}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows by Lemma
4.9
.

P_{2}=M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\lambda x.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction
M_{3}
exists such that

\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\lambda x.M_{2}
; this is impossible.

P_{2}=\mu\gamma.M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{2}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there are
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then
M_{3}=\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and

M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}

M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}

by Proposition
4.11
(
1
).
Then
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{1}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}

\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}

M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{3}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}

\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}

M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{3}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}

follows by Lemma
4.9
.

(11)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}[\hskip 0.25pt{M_{0}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\nu x.M_{1}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
5
), either:

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}[M_{2}]N_{2}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

Then by induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
\nu x.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
3
),
M_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}\nu x.M_{2}^{\prime}
and
M_{3}\mathrel{\equiv}\nu x.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
.
Since
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\nu x.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{3}
, by Rule
(11)
,
[M_{2}]N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
We have
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
by Lemma
4.9
.

P_{2}=M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\nu x.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
\nu x.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\nu x.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
3
),
M_{3}=\nu x.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
.
Then
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows by Lemma
4.9
.

P_{2}=\mu\gamma.M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N_{2}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists

M_{3}
such that
\nu x.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
; this is impossible.

(12)
:

Then
P_{0}\mathrel{\equiv}[\hskip 0.25pt{M_{0}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{\equiv}P_{1}
because
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{1}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{1}
.
By Proposition
4.11
(
5
), either:

P_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}[M_{2}]N_{2}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

Then by induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
3
),
M_{2}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{2}^{\prime}
and
M_{3}\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
.
Since
M_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
and
N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{3}
, by Rule
(11)
,
[M_{2}]N_{2}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
.
We have
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
by Lemma
4.9
.

P_{2}=M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\nu x.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}

such that
\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\nu x.M_{2}
; this is impossible.

P_{2}=M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
with
M_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
and
N_{0}\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}}N_{2}
:

By induction there exists
M_{3}
,
N_{3}
such that
\mu\alpha.M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}\mu\alpha.M_{2}
, and
N_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{3}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}N_{2}
; then by Proposition
4.11
(
3
),
M_{3}=\mu\alpha.M_{3}^{\prime}
, and
M_{1}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}
; then
M_{1}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{1}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.5pt\raise 7.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-60.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{3}^{\prime}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{3}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathop{\kern-1.0pt\raise 10.0pt\hbox{\rotatebox{-120.0}{$\mathbin{\Rightarrow}$}}}M_{2}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN_{2}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
follows by Lemma
4.9
.∎

We can now state our main result.

Theorem 4.15

(Confluence)
.

Reduction in
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is confluent.

Proof 4.16
.

By Theorem
4.13
, we have that
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
satisfies the diamond property, and by Lemma
4.7
that
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}^{*}_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is the transitive closure of
\mathrel{{\Rightarrow}\kern-1.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.
Then by Definition
4.1
,
\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is confluent.
∎

5.
Representing
\vdash_{\textsc{ni}}
in
\vdash_{\mathcal{L}}

In this section we will show that all statements provable in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
have a witness in
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.
We achieve this result by first defining a mapping for terms from
\nu\lambda\mu
to
{\mathcal{L}}
; this will deal with a necessary transformation of derivations when establishing a relation between typeability in
\nu\lambda\mu
and
{\mathcal{L}}
.
What we use here is the transformation from
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
to
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
:

({\textsl{Ax}})

{y\mathord{:}\neg C}\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{\neg C}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.37073pt})

{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{[M]y}:{{\perp}}

(\mu)

{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{y}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.73125pt{{\lceil}\hskip 0.975pt{[M]y}\hskip 0.975pt{\rfloor}}}:{C}

into

({\textsl{Ax}})

{y\mathord{:}C\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{C}}

(\textsl{N})

{y\mathord{:}C,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]y}:{{\perp}}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.3253pt})

{\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.20332pt{.}\hskip 0.20332pt[\alpha]y}:{\neg C}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2259pt{M}\hskip 0.2259pt]\hskip 1.3555pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259pt[\alpha]y})}:{{\perp}}}

(\mu)

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt{\hbox{{{C}}}\hskip 0.71297pt{{\lceil}\hskip 0.95062pt{[\hskip 0.23766pt{M}\hskip 0.23766pt]\hskip 1.42593pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\alpha]y})}\hskip 0.95062pt{\rfloor}}}}:{C}}

Remark that in the first, there is no subterm that has type
C
, whereas in the second, there is.
So we can deal with
({\textsl{PbC}})
towards an assumption that is not on the left.

Definition 5.1
.

We define a mapping
\mathord{\llceil{\mathop{\cdot}}\rrfloor}_{V}:\nu\lambda\mu\mathop{\rightarrow}\mathcal{L}
inductively over terms.

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{}l}\mathord{\llceil{x}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]x&(\alpha{/}x\mathbin{\in}V)\\
\mathord{\llceil{x}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&x&(x\mathbin{\not\in}V)\\
\mathord{\llceil{\mu\hskip 0.5pt{x}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{V,\alpha{/}x}\end{array}\quad\quad\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathord{\llceil{\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{V}\\
\mathord{\llceil{{M}\hskip 0.5ptN}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&{\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{V}}\hskip 0.5pt\mathord{\llceil{N}\rrfloor}_{V}\\
\mathord{\llceil{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptM}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{V}\\
\mathord{\llceil{[\hskip 0.25pt{M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&[\hskip 0.25pt{\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{V}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5pt\mathord{\llceil{N}\rrfloor}_{V}\\
\end{array}

and define

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\quad}l}\underline{x}&=&\varnothing\\
\underline{\lambda x.M}=\underline{\nu x.M}&=&\underline{M}\\
\underline{MN}=\underline{[M]N}&=&\underline{M}\mathrel{\cup}\underline{N}\\
\underline{\mu x.M}&=&\underline{M}\mathrel{\cup}\{\hskip 0.25pt\hbox{$\alpha{/}x$}\hskip 0.25pt\}&(\alpha\textit{ fresh})\\
\end{array}\quad\quad\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\quad}l}\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}A}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma}\rrfloor}_{V},\alpha\mathord{:}A&(\alpha{/}x\mathbin{\in}V)\\
\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}A}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma}\rrfloor}_{V},x\mathord{:}A&(\alpha{/}x\mathbin{\not\in}V)\\
\mathord{\llceil{\varnothing}\rrfloor}_{V}&=&\varnothing\end{array}

Remark that, if
\alpha{/}x\mathbin{\in}V
then
x
was bound under
\mu
, so in a derivation will have a negated type.
Notice that
all
occurrences of term variables that occur in
V
are replaced by
\mathord{\llceil{\mathop{\cdot}}\rrfloor}_{V}
, even if they appear on the left in
({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
: that this is not problematic, can be illustrated by the following:

\begin{array}[t]{lclclcl}\mathord{\llceil{\mu y.[\hskip 0.25pt{y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptM}\rrfloor}_{\epsilon}&=&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{\mathord{\llceil{y}\rrfloor}_{\alpha/y}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5pt\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{\alpha/y}}&=&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]y}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5pt\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{\alpha/y}}&\mathrel{{\rightarrow}\kern-2.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}&\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5pt\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{\alpha/y}}\end{array}

so the substitutions on the left-hand side do not affect the result, but just create a slightly more complicated proof than would be necessary.

We can now show a representation result, which essentially shows that, although the inference rules of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
and
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
differ significantly in their applicability of the rule
(\mu)
, which represents the proof rule
({\textsl{PbC}})
, they can witness the same results in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
.
This result does not establish a rule-to-rule mapping of the correspondence between the systems, but states that logical judgements that are provable in one system are also provable in the other.
We know that every provable judgement in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
corresponds directly to a provable statement in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
, and vice versa, and with the correspondence we show here, we get that this also holds between
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
and
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
.

We first establish a relation between typeability in
\nu\lambda\mu
and
{\mathcal{L}}
.

Theorem 5.2
.

(1)

If
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
, then
{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}{M}:{A}
.

(2)

If
{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}{M}:{A}
, and
V=\underline{M}
, then
{\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma}\rrfloor}_{V}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{V}}:{A}}
.

Proof 5.3
.

(1)

Since, once allowing Greek characters for variables as well, rule
(\textsl{N})
can be omitted and
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is a sub-inference system of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
.

(2)

By induction on the definition of
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}
.

({\textsl{Ax}})
:

Then
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\kern-2.5pt,x\mathord{:}A
; we have two cases:

\alpha{/}x\mathbin{\in}V
:

Then
A=\neg B
,
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\kern-2.5pt,x\mathord{:}\neg B
, so
\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma}\rrfloor}_{V}=\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma^{\prime}}\rrfloor}_{V},\alpha\mathord{:}\neg B
and
\mathord{\llceil{x}\rrfloor}_{V}=\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt[\alpha]x
.
We can derive:

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma^{\prime}}\rrfloor}_{V},x\mathord{:}B}\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{B}

(\textsl{N})

{\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma^{\prime}}\rrfloor}_{V},\alpha\mathord{:}\neg B,x\mathord{:}B}\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]x}:{{\perp}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.38998pt})

{\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma^{\prime}}\rrfloor}_{V},\alpha\mathord{:}\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]x}:{\neg B}

x\mathbin{\not\in}V
:

Then
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\kern-2.5pt,x\mathord{:}A
, so
x\mathord{:}A\mathbin{\in}\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma}\rrfloor}_{V}
and
\mathord{\llceil{x}\rrfloor}_{V}=x
; the result follows by rule
({\textsl{Ax}})
.

(\mu)
:

Then
M=\mu x.N
,
\mathord{\llceil{M}\rrfloor}_{V}=\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mathord{\llceil{N}\rrfloor}_{V,\alpha/x}
and the derivation for
{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\nu\lambda\mu}}{M}:{A}
is shaped like:

(\mu)

{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{x}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374ptN}:{A}

By induction we have
{\mathord{\llceil{\Gamma}\rrfloor}_{V},\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{\mathord{\llceil{N}\rrfloor}_{V,\alpha/x}}:{{\perp}}}
; the result follows by rule
(\mu)
.

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 1.0pt})
,
({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
,
({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt})
, and
({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
:

Straightforward by induction.
∎

Moreover, we now have have that every provable judgement in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
can be inhabited in
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.

Corollary 5.4
.

If
\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}A
, if and only if there exist
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
M\mathbin{\in}{\mathcal{L}}
such that
\overline{\Gamma^{\prime}}=\Gamma
and
{\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
.

We will illustrate the expressiveness of
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.

Example 5.5
.

We can witness
(\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A)\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathop{\rightarrow}B
in
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
(let
\Gamma=x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A,y\mathord{:}A,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg B
):

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.7962pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{z}:{B}}

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.81662pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]z}:{{\perp}}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.335pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.20938pt{.}\hskip 0.20938pt[\alpha]z}:{\neg B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.35242pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{{x}\hskip 0.44054pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026pt[\alpha]z})}:{\neg A}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{A}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2259pt{{x}\hskip 0.45183pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.2259pt]\hskip 0.45183pty}:{{\perp}}}

(\mu)

{x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A,y\mathord{:}A\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.46342pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt{[\hskip 0.2317pt{{x}\hskip 0.46342pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.2317pt]\hskip 0.46342pty}}:{B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})

{x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt{[\hskip 0.23766pt{{x}\hskip 0.47531pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.23766pt]\hskip 0.47531pty}}:{A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

{\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda xy\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt{[\hskip 0.24374pt{{x}\hskip 0.4875pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 0.4875pty}}:{(\neg B\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg A)\mathop{\rightarrow}A\mathop{\rightarrow}B}}

We can show Mendelson’s Axiom 3 in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
.
Let
\Gamma=\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A,\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.85904pt,\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.83755pt,\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg B}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.35242pt})

{\Gamma\kern-0.88107pt,\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg A}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.83755pt,\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.81662pt,\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{\neg B}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.3436pt})

{\Gamma\kern-0.85904pt,\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{A}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})

{\Gamma\kern-0.90367pt,\neg B}\mathrel{\vdash}{{\perp}}

({\textsl{PbC}})

{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{B}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})

{\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{(}\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A)\mathord{\rightarrow}B

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

{}\mathrel{\vdash}{(\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A})\mathord{\rightarrow}(\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A)\mathord{\rightarrow}B

This proof gets represented in
\nu\lambda\mu
by the term
\lambda xy\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt\mu\hskip 0.5pt{z}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25pt{[\hskip 0.25pt{{x}\hskip 0.5ptz}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 1.5pt({{y}\hskip 0.5ptz})}
.

Interpreting this into
{\mathcal{L}}
gives (where
\Gamma=x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A,y\mathord{:}\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg B
):

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{x}:{\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.7962pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{z}:{B}}

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.81662pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]z}:{{\perp}}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.335pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.20938pt{.}\hskip 0.20938pt[\alpha]z}:{\neg B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.35242pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{{x}\hskip 0.44054pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.22026pt{.}\hskip 0.22026pt[\alpha]z})}:{\neg A}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{y}:{\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A}}

({\textsl{Ax}})

{\Gamma\kern-0.77629pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{z}:{B}}

(\textsl{N})

{\Gamma\kern-0.7962pt,z\mathord{:}B\mathrel{\vdash}{[\alpha]z}:{{\perp}}}

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.32663pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.20415pt{.}\hskip 0.20415pt[\alpha]z}:{\neg B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.3436pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{{y}\hskip 0.42952pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.21475pt{.}\hskip 0.21475pt[\alpha]z})}:{A}}

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.36145pt})

{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{[\hskip 0.2259pt{{x}\hskip 0.45183pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.2259pt]\hskip 1.3555pt({{y}\hskip 0.45183pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.2259pt{.}\hskip 0.2259pt[\alpha]z})})}:{{\perp}}}

(\mu)

{\Gamma{\setminus}\alpha\mathrel{\vdash}{\mu\hskip 0.46342pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt{[\hskip 0.2317pt{{x}\hskip 0.46342pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.2317pt]\hskip 1.39027pt({{y}\hskip 0.46342pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.2317pt{.}\hskip 0.2317pt[\alpha]z})})}}:{B}}

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.95062pt})

{x\mathord{:}\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda y\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt\mu\hskip 0.47531pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt{[\hskip 0.23766pt{{x}\hskip 0.47531pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.23766pt]\hskip 1.42593pt({{y}\hskip 0.47531pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.23766pt{.}\hskip 0.23766pt[\alpha]z})})}}:{(}}\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A)\mathord{\rightarrow}B

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 0.975pt})

{\mathrel{\vdash}{\lambda xy\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt\mu\hskip 0.4875pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt{[\hskip 0.24374pt{{x}\hskip 0.4875pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]z})}\hskip 0.24374pt]\hskip 1.46251pt({{y}\hskip 0.4875pt({\mbox{{$\nu$}}z\hskip 0.24374pt{.}\hskip 0.24374pt[\alpha]z})})}}:{(\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}\neg A}})\mathord{\rightarrow}(\neg B\mathord{\rightarrow}A)\mathord{\rightarrow}B

6.
Strong normalisation

In this section we shall prove that every term typeable in
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is strongly normalisable; this will be done using the technique of
reducibility candidates
, as first defined by Girard
[
Gir71
]
, based on work by Tait
[
Tai67
]
.
We will follow Parigot’s application
[
Par97
]
of the reducibility method, but adapted to negation and applications like
[M]N
.

Definition 6.1
.

(1)

We use
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M})
to express that
M
is strongly normalisable (all reduction paths starting from
M
are of finite length), and
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}=\{\,\hbox{$M\mathbin{\in}{\mathcal{L}}\mid\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M})$}\,\}
.

(2)

As in
[
Par97
]
, we write
V^{\textsc{f}}
for the set of all finite sequences of elements of
V
, with
\epsilon
representing the empty sequence, and use the notation

\rightharpoonup
v
for elements of
V^{\textsc{f}}
.

Proposition 6.2
.

The following properties hold of
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
:

(1)

\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({xM_{1}\dots M_{n}})
(with
n\geq 0
) and
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M^{\prime}})
if and only if
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({xM_{1}\dots M_{n}M^{\prime}})
.

(2)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M})
, then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({\lambda x.M})
.

(3)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M})
, then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({\nu x.M})
.

(4)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M})
then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({\mu\alpha.M})
.

(5)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({Mx})
then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M})
.

(6)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.80904pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.80904pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$P$}})
and
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({N})
, then

(7)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.80904pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.80904pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$P$}})
and
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({N})
, then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({[\hskip 0.25pt{\nu x.M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.80904pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.80904pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$P$}})
.

(8)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{N}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.80904pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.80904pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$P$}})
and
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({N})
, then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({({\mu\alpha.M})\hskip 0.5ptN\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.80904pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.80904pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$P$}})
.

(9)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptN{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.80904pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.80904pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$P$}})
and
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({N})
, then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({[\hskip 0.25pt{\mu\alpha.M}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptN\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.80904pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.80904pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$P$}})
.

(10)

If
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M[\beta/\alpha]})
, then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({[\hskip 0.25pt{\beta}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 1.5pt({\mu\alpha.M})})
.

The idea is to assign to each type
A
a set of strongly normalisable terms
\textit{Red}\,(A)
, and to show that every term typeable with
A
is an element of
\textit{Red}\,(A)
, and thereby strongly normalisable.
Central to the predicate is the notion of functional construction, which states that a set is in
\textit{Red}\,(A\mathord{\rightarrow}B)
, if it is contained of terms that return terms in
\textit{Red}\,(B)
when applied to terms in
\textit{Red}\,(A)
.

The latter is expressed through the following:

{defiC}

[
[
Par97
]
]

(1)

The
functional construction

\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}:\wp\,{\mathcal{L}}\mathbin{\times}\wp\,{\mathcal{L}}\mathop{\rightarrow}\wp\,{\mathcal{L}}
is defined through:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}A\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}B&\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}&\{\,\hbox{$M\mathbin{\in}{\mathcal{L}}\mid\forall\,N\mathbin{\in}A\,(\hskip 0.5pt{M}\hskip 0.5ptN\mathbin{\in}B\hskip 0.5pt)$}\,\}\end{array}

(2)

\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}
is generalised to
\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}
through:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}A\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}B&\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}&\{\,\hbox{$M\mathbin{\in}{\mathcal{L}}\mid\forall\,\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}\mathbin{\in}A^{\textsc{f}}\,(\hskip 0.5pt{M}\hskip 0.5pt\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}\mathbin{\in}B\hskip 0.5pt)$}\,\}\end{array}

Using functional construction, reducibility candidates can easily be defined.

{defiC}

[
[
Par97
]
]
The set
\textit{Red}\subseteq\wp\,{\mathcal{L}}
of
reducibility candidates
is inductively defined through:

(1)

\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}
;

(2)

If
A\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}
and
B\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}
, then
A\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}B\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}
.

We write
\textit{Red}\,(A)
for
A\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}
.

The next result states that all terms that are reducible in
A
are also strongly normalisable, and that all variables are reducible in any type.

{lemC}

[
[
Par97
]
]
If
\textit{Red}\,(A)
, then
A\subseteq\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
and
A
contains the
\lambda
-variables.

Proof 6.3
.

We prove

(1)
A\subseteq\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
and
(2) for all
\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}^{\textsc{f}}
,
x\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}\mathbin{\in}A

by induction on the definition of Red.

(1)

A=\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
:

Immediate.

A=B\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}C
:

Take
M\mathbin{\in}A
, then

\forall\,N\mathbin{\in}A\,(\hskip 0.5pt{M}\hskip 0.5ptN\mathbin{\in}B\hskip 0.5pt)
, so
\forall\,N\mathbin{\in}A\,(\hskip 0.5pt\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({{M}\hskip 0.5ptN})\hskip 0.5pt)
by induction (1).
Take the
\lambda
-variable
x
, then by induction (2),
x\mathbin{\in}B
and therefore
Mx\mathbin{\in}C
, so by induction
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({Mx})
, and therefore by Proposition
6.2
(
5
)
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({M})
.

(2)

A=\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
:

By Proposition
6.2
(
1
).

A=B\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}C
:

Let
N^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}B
, then
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({N^{\prime}})
by induction (1).
Take

\rightharpoonup
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({N_{i}})
, then by Proposition
6.2
(
1
)
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({x\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}N^{\prime}})
, and
x\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}N^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}C
by induction (2).
So
x\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}\mathbin{\in}A
.
∎

{lemC}

[
[
Par97
]
]
If
\textit{Red}\,(A)
, then there exists
B\subseteq\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}^{\textsc{f}}
such that
A=B\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
.

Proof 6.4
.

By induction on the definition of Red.

A=\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
:

Notice that
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\mathrel{\hskip 1.0pt\hbox{\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$=$}\kern-3.8889pt\kern-3.28473pt\raise 4.25pt\hbox{\hbox{{\scriptsize$\Delta$}}}\kern-3.28473pt\kern 3.8889pt}\hskip 1.0pt}\{\hskip 0.25pt\hbox{$\epsilon$}\hskip 0.25pt\}\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
.

A=C\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}D
:

By induction we have
D=E\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
for some
E
, and therefore
A=F\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
where

\begin{array}[]{rcl}F&=&\{\,\hbox{$M\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}\mid M\mathbin{\in}C,\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{9.12497pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-9.12497pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N$}\mathbin{\in}E$}\,\}.\end{array}

∎

Definition 6.5
.

For every
A\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}
,
A^{{\perp}}
is defined as the greatest
B\subseteq\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}^{\textsc{f}}
such that
A=B\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
.

Notice that, since
A\subseteq\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
, if
M\mathbin{\in}A
, then
M\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
and
M\epsilon\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
, so
\epsilon\mathbin{\in}A^{{\perp}}
.

Parigot remarks that membership of
\epsilon
is essential.
He says “
It allows to go from an arbitrary reducibility candidate
A
to
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
and back, without knowing anything about
A
. This property is used for the case of the rule where one switches from one formula to another. Contrary to the case of a
\lambda
where one knows that the type is an arrow, in the one of a
\mu
one has an arbitrary type, but it can be considered as some kind of arrow
A^{{\perp}}\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}^{\textsc{f}}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
whose number of arguments is unknown (possibly zero).
”
[
Par97
]
(notation adapted).

It is perhaps worthwhile to point out that, so far, there is no relation between typeability and reducibility, in that
\textit{Red}\,(A)
does not just contain terms that are typeable with
A
; for example, the term
xx
is in
\textit{Red}\,(A)
, for any
A
, since
xx\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
, but is not typeable in
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.
