However, assume that term
M
is typeable with
A
, then we have a derivation that shows
{\mathcal{D}}:{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
and for every free variable
x
in
M
there will a type
B
such that
x\mathord{:}B\mathbin{\in}\Gamma
, and
\mathcal{D}
contains occurrences of rule
({\textsl{Ax}})
showing
{\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{x}:{B}}
.
We will be capable of proving that then replacing
x
in
M
by elements of
\textit{Red}\,(B)
, for every free variable of
M
, creates an element of
\textit{Red}\,(A)
and this will be sufficient for our purposes.

In
[
Par97
]
, Parigot shows termination for typeable terms in
{\lambda\mu}
enriched with quantification rules; in order to deal with the binding of type variables, he defines a notion of type interpretation that maps type variables onto reducible sets, extended naturally to quantification and (using functional construction) to arrow types.
Here we do not need to deal with quantification, but find it convenient to continue on his path.

Definition 6.6
.

An
interpretation

\xi
is a function from type variables to Red.
Interpretations are extended to arbitrary formulas by:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\xi(A\mathop{\rightarrow}B)&=&\xi(A)\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}\xi(B)\\
\xi(\neg A)&=&\xi(A)\mathrel{{\rightharpoondown}\kern-10.00002pt\raise 1.5pt\hbox{$\rightharpoonup$}}\xi({\perp})\\
\xi({\perp})&=&\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\end{array}

We shall now prove our strong normalisation result by showing that every term typeable with
A
is reducible in that type.
For this, we need to prove a stronger property:
we will now show that if we replace term-variables by reducible terms in a typeable term, then we obtain a reducible term.

Lemma 6.7

(Replacement Lemma)
.

•

Let
\Gamma=\{\,\hbox{$x_{1}{:}B_{1},\ldots,x_{n}{:}B_{n},\alpha_{1}\mathord{:}\neg C_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}\mathord{:}\neg C_{m}$}\,\}
.

•

Let for
1\mathbin{\leq}{i}\mathbin{\leq}{n}
,
N_{i}\mathbin{\in}\xi(B_{i})
, and
for all
1\mathbin{\leq}{j}\mathbin{\leq}{m}
,
\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-6.80557pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L$}_{j}\mathbin{\in}\xi(C_{j})^{{\perp}}
if
C_{j}=D_{j}\mathord{\rightarrow}E_{j}
, and

L_{j}\mathbin{\in}\xi(D_{j})
if
C_{j}=\neg D_{j}
.

•

Let
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ_{j}?{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
stand for
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}_{j}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma_{j}}{/}\alpha_{j}\hskip-0.5pt\}
if
C_{j}=(D_{j}\mathord{\rightarrow}E_{j})
, or
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ_{j}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}
if
C_{j}=\neg D_{j}
.

If
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
, then
M\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\kern 1.0pt\raise 7.5pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{26.49883pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-26.49883pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N_{i}{/}x_{i}$}\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\kern 1.0pt\raise 7.5pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{29.5834pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-29.5834pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L_{i}?{/}\alpha_{i}$}\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathbin{\in}\xi(A)
.

Proof 6.8
.

By induction on the structure of derivations. We will use
S
for
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\kern 1.0pt\raise 7.5pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{26.49883pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-26.49883pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$N_{i}{/}x_{i}$}\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt\kern 1.0pt\raise 7.5pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{29.5834pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-29.5834pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L_{i}?{/}\alpha_{i}$}\hskip-0.5pt\}
.

({\textsl{Ax}})
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}x_{i}
, for some
1\mathbin{\leq}{j}\mathbin{\leq}{n}
,
B_{i}=A
, and
M\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathrel{\equiv}x_{i}\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathrel{\equiv}N_{i}
.
From the second assumption we have that
N_{i}\mathbin{\in}\xi(A)
.

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 1.0pt})
:

Then
M=\lambda x.N
,
A=F\mathord{\rightarrow}G
and
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}F\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{G}}
.
Let
P\mathbin{\in}\xi(F)
, then by Lemma
6

\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({P})
, and by induction
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(G)
.
Let
\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}\mathbin{\in}\xi(G)^{{\perp}}
, then
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptP{/}x\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
by Definition
6.5
.
Then by Proposition
6.2
(
6
) also
(\lambda x.N)P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
.
Therefore, by Definition
6.5
,
(\lambda x.N)P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(G)
and since
S
does not affect
P
, also
({\lambda x.N)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}P\mathbin{\in}\xi(G})\hskip 0.5pt
; then by Definition
6
and
6.6
,
(\lambda x.N)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(F\mathord{\rightarrow}G)
.

({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
:

Then
M=PQ
and there exists
F
such that
{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{P}:{F\mathord{\rightarrow}A}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{Q}:{F}}
.
By induction,
P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(F\mathord{\rightarrow}A)
and
Q\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(F)
; by Definition
6
and
6.6
we have
{P}\hskip 0.5pt\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\,Q\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(A)
, and
{P}\hskip 0.5pt\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\,Q\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathrel{\equiv}({P}\hskip 0.5ptQ)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}
.

({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt})
:

Then
M\mathrel{\equiv}\nu y.P
,
A=\neg F
, and
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,y\mathord{:}F\mathrel{\vdash}{P}:{{\perp}}}
.
Assume
Q\mathbin{\in}\xi(F)
, then by induction,
P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathbin{\in}\xi({\perp})
, so by Definition
6.6
,
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5ptQ{/}y\hskip-0.5pt\}})
.
Then by Proposition
6.2
(
7
), we have
\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}\hskip 0.4pt({[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ})
, so by Definition
6.6
,
[\hskip 0.25pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ\mathbin{\in}\xi({\perp})
, so by Definition
6.6

\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(\neg F)
, and
\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathrel{\equiv}(\mbox{{$\nu$}}y\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}
.

({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
:

Then
A={\perp}
,
M\mathrel{\equiv}[\hskip 0.25pt{P}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ
, and there exists
F
such that
{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{P}:{\neg F}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{Q}:{F}}
.
Then, by induction, we have
P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(\neg F)
and
Q\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(F)
.
Then by Definition
6
and
6.6
,
[\hskip 0.25pt{P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi({\perp})
, and
[\hskip 0.25pt{P\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathrel{\equiv}([\hskip 0.25pt{P}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}
.

(\textsl{N})
:

Then
M=[\alpha_{j}]N
with
1\mathbin{\leq}{j}\mathbin{\leq}{m}
,
A={\perp}
, and
{\Gamma\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{C_{j}}}
with
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C_{j}\mathbin{\in}\Gamma
.
By induction,
N\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(C_{j})
.
Now either:

C_{j}=D_{j}\mathord{\rightarrow}E_{j}
:

Notice that
\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-6.80557pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L$}_{j}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma_{j}}{/}\alpha_{j}\hskip-0.5pt\}\mathbin{\in}\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}
and therefore
([\alpha_{j}]N)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}=[\gamma_{j}]N\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-6.80557pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L$}_{j}
.
We have
\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-6.80557pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L$}_{j}\mathbin{\in}\xi(C_{j})^{{\perp}}
by assumption, and therefore by Definition
6.5
,
N\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-6.80557pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L$}_{j}\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
, so also
[\gamma_{j}](N\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}})\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-6.80557pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L$}_{j}\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
; then by Definition
6.6
,
[\gamma_{j}](N\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}})\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-6.80557pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$L$}_{j}\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}\,({\perp})
.

C_{j}=\neg F
:

Now
V_{j}\mathbin{\in}\xi(F)
by assumption, and therefore by Definition
6.6
,
[\hskip 0.25pt{N\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptV_{j}\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}\,({\perp})
, and since
\{Q/\alpha\}\mathbin{\in}\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}
, also
[\hskip 0.25pt{N\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptV_{j}=([\alpha]N)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}
.

(\mu)
:

Then
M=\mu\beta.N
and
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\beta\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{\vdash}{N}:{{\perp}}}
.
Now either:

A=D\mathord{\rightarrow}E
:

Let
\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}\mathbin{\in}\xi(A)^{{\perp}}
, then by induction
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi({\perp})
and by Definition
6
and
6.6
,
N\hskip 0.75pt\{\hskip-0.5pt{\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}}{\mathop{\cdot}}{\gamma}{/}\alpha\hskip-0.5pt\}\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}
.
Then by Proposition
6.2
(
8
),
(\mu\alpha.N)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\kern 1.0pt\raise 6.83331pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{7.90555pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-7.90555pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$Q$}\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}

(\mu\alpha.N)\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}\mathbin{\in}\xi(A)
.

A=\neg D
:

Assume

, then by induction

and by Definition

,

.

, we have
[\hskip 0.25pt{\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptP\hskip 0.5pt{\textbf{S}}}\hskip 0.25pt]\hskip 0.5ptQ\mathbin{\in}\mathcal{S\kern-1.0ptN}

, so by Definition

.

We can now prove the main result.

Theorem 6.9

(Strong Normalisation)
.

Any term typeable in ‘
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
’ is strongly normalisable.

Proof 6.10
.

Let
\Gamma=x_{1}{:}B_{1},\ldots,x_{n}{:}B_{n},\alpha_{1}\mathord{:}\neg C_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m}\mathord{:}\neg C_{m}
such that
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
.
Then by Lemma
6
, for all
1\mathbin{\leq}{i}\mathbin{\leq}{n}
,
x_{i}\mathbin{\in}\xi(B_{i})
and
\epsilon\mathbin{\in}\xi(C_{j})^{{\perp}}
.
Then, by Lemma
6.7
,
M\,\{\kern 1.0pt\raise 7.5pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{25.3113pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-25.3113pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$x_{i}/x_{i}$}\}\{\kern 1.0pt\raise 7.5pt\hbox{\rule[1.5pt]{28.8362pt}{0.4pt}\kern-6.39732pt\hbox{\tiny$\rightharpoonup$}}\kern-28.8362pt\kern-0.5pt\hbox{$\epsilon?/\alpha_{j}$}\}\mathbin{\in}\textit{Red}\,(A)
; strong normalisation for

then follows from Lemma

.∎

7.
Principal typing for
\mathcal{L}

In this section, we will show that we can define a notion of principal typing for
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.
This is achieved in the standard way: we define notions of type substitutions and unification, that are used for the definition of the algorithm
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt
that calculates the principal typing for each term typeable in
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
.

Substitution is shown to be sound,
i.e.
maps inferable judgements to inferable judgements, and the algorithm is shown to be complete in that all inferable judgements for a term can be constructed from its principal typing.

Definition 7.1

(Substitution and unification)
.

(1)

(a)

The substitution
({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526pt
, where
{\varphi}
is a type variable and
C
a type, is inductively defined
8
8

8

All algorithmic definitions in this section are presented in ‘functional style’, where calls are matched against the alternatives ‘top-down’, the first match is taken, and the result is undefined in case there is no match.
by:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526pt{\perp}&=&{\perp}\\
({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526pt{\varphi}&=&C\\
({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526pt{\varphi}^{\prime}&=&{\varphi}^{\prime}\hfill({\varphi}^{\prime}\not={\varphi})\\
({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526ptA\mathop{\rightarrow}B&=&(({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526ptA)\rightarrow(({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526ptB)\\
({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526pt\neg A&=&\neg(({\varphi}\mapsto C)\hskip 2.84526ptA)\end{array}

(b)

If
\textit{S}_{1}
,
\textit{S}_{2}
are substitutions, then so is
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{2}
, where
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{A}=\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 0.5pt(\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{A})
.

(c)

\textit{S}\,{\Gamma}=\{\,\hbox{$x\mathord{:}\textit{S}\,{B}\mid x\mathord{:}B\mathbin{\in}\Gamma$}\,\}\mathrel{\cup}\{\,\hbox{$\alpha\mathord{:}\textit{S}\,{B}\mid\alpha\mathord{:}B\mathbin{\in}\Gamma$}\,\}
.

(d)

\textit{S}\,{{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt{\Gamma}\,,\,{A}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}}
=
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt{\textit{S}\,{\Gamma}}\,,\,{\textit{S}\,{A}}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.

(e)

If there exists a substitution
S
such that
\textit{S}\,{A}=B
, then
B
is a (substitution) instance of
A
.

(f)

\textit{Id}_{S}
is the identity substitution that replaces all type variables by themselves.

(2)

Unification of types is defined by:

\begin{array}[]{lllcl}\textit{unify}&{\varphi}&{\varphi}&=&({\varphi}\mapsto{\varphi})\\
\textit{unify}&{\varphi}&B&=&({\varphi}\mapsto B)\quad({\varphi}\textit{ does not occur in }B)\\
\textit{unify}&A&{\varphi}&=&\textit{unify}\ {\varphi}\ A\\
\textit{unify}&(A\mathop{\rightarrow}B)&(C\mathop{\rightarrow}D)&=&\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{1}\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt\textrm{where}\lx@intercol&\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\textit{S}_{1}&=&\textit{unify}\ A\ C\\
\textit{S}_{2}&=&\textit{unify}\ (\ \textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{B})(\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{D})\end{array}\\
\textit{unify}&(\neg A)&(\neg C)&=&\textit{unify}\ A\ C\end{array}

(3)

The operation
unifyC
generalises
unify
to contexts:

\begin{array}[]{lllcl}\textit{unifyC}&(\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1},x\mathord{:}A)&(\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2},x\mathord{:}B)&=&\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{1},\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt\textrm{where}\lx@intercol&\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\textit{S}_{1}&=&\textit{unify}\ A\ B\\
\textit{S}_{2}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1}})\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2}})\end{array}\\
\textit{unifyC}&(\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1},x\mathord{:}A)&\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ \Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1}\ \Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2}\quad(x\mathbin{\not\in}\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2})\\
\textit{unifyC}&(\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1},\alpha\mathord{:}A)&(\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2},\alpha\mathord{:}B)&=&\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{1},\\
\lx@intercol\hfil\hskip 8.53581pt\textrm{where}\lx@intercol&\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\textit{S}_{1}&=&\textit{unify}\ A\ B\\
\textit{S}_{2}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1}})\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2}})\end{array}\\
\textit{unifyC}&(\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1},\alpha\mathord{:}A)&\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ \Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{1}\ \Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2}\quad(\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2})\\
\textit{unifyC}&\emptyset&\Gamma\kern-1.5pt_{2}&=&\textit{Id}_{S}\end{array}

This definition specifies
unify
as a partial function; if the side condition ‘
{\varphi}

does not occur in

B
’ fails, no result is returned.
So, for example, ‘
\textit{unify}\ {\varphi}\ {\varphi}\mathop{\rightarrow}{\varphi}
’ or ‘
\textit{unify}\ (\ A\mathord{\rightarrow}B){\,\neg(C\mathord{\rightarrow}D)}
’ does not return a substitution.

If successful, unification returns the most general unifier, as stated by:

{propC}

[
[
Rob65
]
]
For all
A
,
B
: if
\textit{S}_{1}
is a substitution such that
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{A}=\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{B}
(so then
\textit{S}_{1}
is a
unifier
of
A
and
B
), then there exist substitutions
\textit{S}_{2}
and
\textit{S}_{3}
such that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\textit{S}_{2}&=&\textit{unify}\ A\ B\end{array}
and

\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\,}}\textit{S}_{1}&=&\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{2}\end{array}
.

Lemma 7.2

(Soundness of substitution)
.

If
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
, then
{\textit{S}\,{\Gamma}\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{\textit{S}\,{A}}}
.

Proof 7.3
.

By straightforward induction on the structure of derivations.∎

We now define a notion of principal typing for terms of
\mathcal{L}
.

Definition 7.4
.

The principal typing algorithm for
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}
is given in Figure
1
.

\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{x}~=~{}\langle\hskip 0.5ptx\mathord{:}{\varphi}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}

where
\begin{array}[t]{rcl}{\varphi}&\textit{is}&\textit{fresh}\end{array}

\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\lambda x.M}~=~{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}

where
\begin{array}[t]{rcl}{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{M}\\
\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~}l}\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}A\mathop{\rightarrow}P^{\prime}&(x\mathord{:}A\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime})\\
\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}\mathop{\rightarrow}P^{\prime}&(x\mathbin{\not\in}\Pi^{\prime})\end{array}\right.\\
{\varphi}&\textit{is}&\textit{fresh}\end{array}

\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{MN}~=~\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\cup}\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}}

where
\begin{array}[t]{rcl@{\quad}l}{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{1}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{M}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{2}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{N}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
\textit{S}_{1}&=&\textit{unify}\ P_{1}\ P_{2}\mathop{\rightarrow}{\varphi}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
\textit{S}_{2}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}})\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}})\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
{\varphi}&\textit{is}&\textit{fresh}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\end{array}

\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\nu x.M}~=~{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}

where
\begin{array}[t]{rcl}{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{M}\\
\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~}l}\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}\neg A&(x\mathord{:}A\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime})\\
\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}\neg{\varphi}&(x\mathbin{\not\in}\Pi^{\prime})\end{array}\right.\\
{\varphi}&\textit{is}&\textit{fresh}\end{array}

\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{[M]N}~=~\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\cup}\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}}

where
\begin{array}[t]{rcl@{\quad}l}{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{1}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{M}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{2}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{N}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
\textit{S}_{1}&=&\textit{unify}\ P_{1}\ \neg P_{2}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
\textit{S}_{2}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}})\ (\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}})\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\end{array}

\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\mu\alpha.M}~=~{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}

where
\begin{array}[t]{rcl}{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{M}\\
\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~}l}\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}\alpha\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}A&(\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime})\\
\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}&(\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}\Pi^{\prime})\end{array}\right.\\
{\varphi}&\textit{is}&\textit{fresh}\end{array}

\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{[\alpha]N}~=~{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}

where
\begin{array}[t]{rcl@{\quad}l}{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle&=&\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{N}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
\Pi&=&\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~}l}\textit{S}\,{\Pi^{\prime}}&(\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime})\\
\Pi^{\prime},\alpha\mathord{:}\neg P^{\prime}&(\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}\Pi^{\prime})\\
\end{array}\right.\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
\textit{S}&=&\textit{unify}\ A\ P^{\prime}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\\
\end{array}

Figure 1
.
The algorithm
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt

We can show that the algorithm creates valid judgements:

Lemma 7.5

(Soundness of
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt
)
.

If
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{M}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}

{\Pi\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{P}}
.

Proof 7.6
.

By induction on the structure of terms, using Lemma
7.2
.

We will now show the main result for
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt
, which states that it calculates the most general typeing with respect to type substitution for all terms typeable in
\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}

Theorem 7.7

(Completeness of substitution.)
.

If
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{M}:{A}}
, then there exists context
\Pi
, type
P
, and substitution
S
such that:
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{M}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
,
\textit{S}\,{\Pi}\subseteq\Gamma
, and
\textit{S}\,{P}=A
.

Proof 7.8
.

By induction on the structure of terms in
\mathcal{L}
.

M\mathrel{\equiv}x
:

Then, by rule
({\textsl{Ax}})
,
x\mathord{:}A\mathbin{\in}\Gamma
, and
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{x}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\{\hskip 0.25pt\hbox{$x\mathord{:}{\varphi}$}\hskip 0.25pt\}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.
Take
\textit{S}=({\varphi}\mapsto A)
.

M\mathrel{\equiv}\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN
:

Then, by rule
({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{I}\hskip 1.0pt})
, there are
C,D
such that
A=C\mathop{\rightarrow}D
, and
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{N}:{D}}
.
Then, by induction, there are
\Pi^{\prime},P^{\prime}
and
\textit{S}^{\prime}
such that
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{N}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
,
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C
, and
\textit{S}\,{P^{\prime}}=D
.
Then either:

x\mathbin{\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Then
x\mathord{:}C^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime}
, and
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}C^{\prime}\mathord{\rightarrow}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.
Since
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C
, in particular
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5ptC^{\prime}=C
,
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt(\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x)\subseteq\Gamma
, and
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt(C^{\prime}\mathord{\rightarrow}P^{\prime})=C\mathop{\rightarrow}D
.
Take
\Pi=\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x
,
P=C^{\prime}\mathord{\rightarrow}P^{\prime}
, and
\textit{S}=\textit{S}^{\prime}
.

x\mathbin{\not\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Then
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}\mathord{\rightarrow}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
,
x
does not occur in
\Pi^{\prime}
, and let
{\varphi}
not occur in
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.
Since
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C
, in particular
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma
.
Take
\textit{S}=\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt({\varphi}\mapsto C)
, then, since
{\varphi}
does not occur in
\Pi^{\prime}
, also
\textit{S}\,{\Pi^{\prime}}\subseteq\Gamma
.
Notice that
\textit{S}\,({{\varphi}\mathord{\rightarrow}P^{\prime}})=C\mathop{\rightarrow}D
; take
\Pi=\Pi^{\prime}
and
P={\varphi}\mathord{\rightarrow}P^{\prime}
.

M\mathrel{\equiv}QR
:

Then, by rule
({\mathord{\rightarrow}\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
, there exists a
B
such that
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{Q}:{B\mathord{\rightarrow}A}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{R}:{B}}
.
By induction, there are
\textit{S}_{1},\textit{S}_{2}
,
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{1}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}=\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{Q}
and
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{2}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}=\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{R}
(no type variables shared) such that
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}}\subseteq\Gamma
,
\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}}\subseteq\Gamma
,
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{P_{1}}=B\mathord{\rightarrow}A
and
\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{P_{2}}=B
.
Notice that
\textit{S}_{1},\textit{S}_{2}
do not interfere.
Let
{\varphi}
be a fresh type variable and

\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\textit{S}_{u}&=&\textit{unify}\ P_{1}\ (P_{2}\mathord{\rightarrow}{\varphi})\\
\textit{S}_{C}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ (\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}})\ (\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}})\\
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{QR}&=&\textit{S}_{C}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\cup}\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}^{\prime}_{1}\mathrel{\cup}\Delta^{\prime}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}}\end{array}

We need to argue that
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{QR}
is successful: since this can only fail on calls to unification (of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}\mathord{\rightarrow}{\varphi}
, or in the unification of the contexts), we need to argue that these are successful.
Take
\textit{S}_{3}=\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt({\varphi}\mapsto A)
, then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 1.5pt{P_{1}}&=&B\mathord{\rightarrow}A,\textrm{ and}\\
\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 0.5pt(P_{2}\mathord{\rightarrow}{\varphi})&=&B\mathord{\rightarrow}A.\end{array}

so
P_{1}
and
P_{2}\mathord{\rightarrow}{\varphi}
have a common instance
B\mathord{\rightarrow}A
, and by Proposition
7
,
\textit{S}_{u}
exists.

Notice that we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}}&\subseteq&\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\textrm{ and}\\
\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}}&\subseteq&\Gamma\end{array}

since
\Pi_{1}
and
\Pi_{2}
share no type-variables.
Since
\Gamma
is a context, each term variable has only one type, and therefore
\textit{S}_{3}
is a unifier for
\Pi_{1}
and
\Pi_{2}
, so we know that an
\textit{S}_{4}
exists which extends the substitution that unifies the contexts, even after being changed with
\textit{S}_{u}
, so such that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\textit{S}_{4}\hskip 0.5pt(\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}})&\subseteq&\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\textrm{ and}\\
\textit{S}_{4}\hskip 0.5pt(\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}})&\subseteq&\Gamma.\end{array}

So
\textit{S}_{4}
also unifies
\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}}
and
\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}}
, so by Proposition
7
there exists a substitution
\textit{S}_{5}
such that
\textit{S}_{4}=\textit{S}_{5}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{\Gamma}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{u}
.
Take
\textit{S}=\textit{S}_{5}
.

M\mathrel{\equiv}\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN
:

Then, by rule
({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt})
, there exists
C
such that
A=\neg C
, and
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{N}:{{\perp}}}
.
Then, by induction, there are
\Pi^{\prime}
and
\textit{S}^{\prime}
such that
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{N}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
, and
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C
.
Then either:

x\mathbin{\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Then
x\mathord{:}C^{\prime}\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime}
, and
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\mbox{{$\nu$}}x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}\neg C^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.
Since
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C
, in particular
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5ptC^{\prime}=C
,
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt(\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x)\subseteq\Gamma
, and
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt(\neg C^{\prime})=\neg C
.
Take
\Pi=\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}x
,
P=\neg C^{\prime}
, and
\textit{S}=\textit{S}^{\prime}
.

x\mathbin{\not\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Then
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\lambda x\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}\neg{\varphi}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
,
x
does not occur in
\Pi^{\prime}
where
{\varphi}
does not occur in
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.
Since
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,x\mathord{:}C
, in particular
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma
.
Take
\textit{S}=\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt({\varphi}\mapsto C)
, then, since
{\varphi}
does not occur in
\Pi^{\prime}
, also
\textit{S}\,{\Pi^{\prime}}\subseteq\Gamma
.
Notice that
\textit{S}\,({\neg{\varphi}})=\neg C
; take
\Pi=\Pi^{\prime}
and
P=\neg{\varphi}
.

M\mathrel{\equiv}[Q]R
:

Then
A={\perp}
and by rule
({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
there exists a
B
such that
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{Q}:{\neg B}}
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{R}:{B}}
.
By induction, there are
\textit{S}_{1},\textit{S}_{2}
,
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{1}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}=\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{Q}
and
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P_{2}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}=\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{R}
(no type variables shared) such that
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}}\subseteq\Gamma
,
\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}}\subseteq\Gamma
,
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{P_{1}}=\neg B
and
\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{P_{2}}=B
.
Notice that
\textit{S}_{1},\textit{S}_{2}
do not interfere.
Let
{\varphi}
be a fresh type variable and

\begin{array}[t]{rcl}\textit{S}_{u}&=&\textit{unify}\ P_{1}\ \neg P_{2}\\
\textit{S}_{C}&=&\textit{unifyC}\ (\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}})\ (\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}})\\
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{QR}&=&\textit{S}_{C}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi_{1}\mathrel{\cup}\Pi_{2}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}}\end{array}

As for the case
M=QR
, take
\textit{S}_{3}=\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt({\varphi}\mapsto A)
, then
\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 1.5pt{P_{1}}=\neg B,
and
\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 1.5pt{P_{2}}=B
, so
P_{1}
and
\neg P_{2}
have a common instance
\neg B
and
\textit{S}_{u}
exists.
Since also
\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}}\subseteq\Gamma
, and
\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}}\subseteq\Gamma
, as above an
\textit{S}_{4}
exists such that

\textit{S}_{4}\hskip 0.5pt(\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{1}})\subseteq\Gamma
, and
\textit{S}_{4}\hskip 0.5pt(\textit{S}_{u}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi_{2}})\subseteq\Gamma
and by Proposition
7
there exists a substitution
\textit{S}_{5}
such that
\textit{S}_{4}=\textit{S}_{5}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{\Gamma}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{u}
.
Take
\textit{S}=\textit{S}_{5}
.

M\mathrel{\equiv}\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN
:

Then, by rule
(\mu)
,
{\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{N}:{{\perp}}}
.
Then, by induction, there are
\Pi^{\prime}
and
\textit{S}^{\prime}
such that
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{N}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
, and
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A
.
Then either:

\alpha\mathbin{\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Then
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime}
, and
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\mu\alpha.N}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}\alpha\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}C\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.
Since
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A
, in particular
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5ptC=A
and
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt(\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}\alpha)\subseteq\Gamma
.
Take
\Pi=\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{\setminus}\alpha
,
P=C^{\prime}
, and
\textit{S}=\textit{S}^{\prime}
.

\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Then
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{\mu\hskip 0.5pt{\alpha}\hskip 0.25pt{.}\hskip 0.25ptN}={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\varphi}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
,
\alpha
does not occur in
\Pi^{\prime}
where
{\varphi}
does not occur in
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
.
Since
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma\kern-1.0pt,\alpha\mathord{:}\neg A
, in particular
\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 1.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma
.
Take
\textit{S}=\textit{S}^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt({\varphi}\mapsto A)
, then, since
{\varphi}
does not occur in
\Pi^{\prime}
, also
\textit{S}\,{\Pi^{\prime}}\subseteq\Gamma
.
Notice that
\textit{S}\,({{\varphi}})=A
; take
\Pi=\Pi^{\prime}
and
P={\varphi}
.

M\mathrel{\equiv}[\alpha]N
:

Then
A={\perp}
and by rule
(\textsl{N})
there exists a
B
such that
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg B\mathbin{\in}\Gamma
and
{\Gamma\mathrel{{\mathrel{\vdash}}\kern-2.5pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}}{N}:{B}}
.
By induction, there exists
\textit{S}_{1}
,
{}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}P^{\prime}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}=\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt{N}
such that
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi^{\prime}}\subseteq\Gamma
, and
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{P^{\prime}}=B
.
Then either:

\alpha\mathbin{\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Let
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime}
; take
\textit{S}_{2}=\textit{unify}\ C\ P^{\prime}
, then
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt[\alpha]N={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi^{\prime}}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
, and
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{C}\mathbin{\in}\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi^{\prime}}
.
Since
\alpha\mathord{:}\neg C\mathbin{\in}\Pi^{\prime}
and
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi^{\prime}}\subseteq\Gamma
, we have that
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\neg C}=\neg B
and
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{P^{\prime}}=B
, so
\textit{S}_{2}
is successful and there exists
\textit{S}_{3}
such that
\textit{S}_{1}=\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 0.5pt{\mathrel{{\circ}}}\hskip 0.5pt\textit{S}_{2}
, so
\textit{S}_{1}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi^{\prime}}=\textit{S}_{3}\hskip 0.5pt(\textit{S}_{2}\hskip 1.5pt{\Pi^{\prime}})\subseteq\Gamma
.
Take
\textit{S}=\textit{S}_{3}
.

\alpha\mathbin{\not\in}\textit{fv}\hskip 0.25pt({N})
:

Then
\textit{pt}\kern-1.0pt_{\mbox{{$\mathcal{L}$}}}\hskip 2.13394pt[\alpha]N={}\langle\hskip 0.5pt\Pi^{\prime},\alpha\mathord{:}\neg P^{\prime}\mathord{{;}\hskip 2.0pt}{\perp}\hskip 0.5pt\rangle{}
; take
\textit{S}=\textit{S}_{1}
.
∎

This last result shows the practicality of our notion of type assignment.

Conclusion and Future Work

We have presented
{\mathcal{L}}
as an extension of Parigot’s
{\lambda\mu}
-calculus by adding negation as a type constructor with the aim of representing proofs in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
, Classical Logic with implication, negation, and Proof by Contradiction.
We gained a more expressive calculus, that no longer represents
\neg A
through
A\mathord{\rightarrow}{\perp}
, but, more importantly, negation elimination is no longer represented by application, and negation introduction not by
\lambda
-abstraction, but through new syntactic constructs that represent negation introduction and elimination directly, thus getting a more faithful representation of proofs in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
.

We defined a notion of reduction that extends
{\lambda\mu}
’s logical and structural reduction rules with two new reduction rules, one dealing with a
({\neg\textsl{I}\hskip 0.4pt}){-}({\neg\textsl{E}\hskip 0.4pt})
-pair, the other when Proof by Contradiction gets applied against an assumption that has a double negated type.
We showed that type assignment is sound, in that assignable types are preserved under reduction.
Using, as suggested by Py, Aczel’s generalisation of Tait and Martin-Löf’s notion of parallel reduction, we showed that reduction is confluent.

By its nature, not all proofs in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
can be represented in
{\mathcal{L}}
, but we have shown a completeness result in that all propositions that can be shown in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
have a witness in
{\mathcal{L}}
.
Following Parigot, using Girard’s approach of reducibility candidates, we have shown that all typeable terms are strongly normalisable, and that type assignment for
{\mathcal{L}}
enjoys the principal typing property.

In all,
{\mathcal{L}}
satisfies all the properties that can be demanded for a calculus claiming to represent proofs and proof contraction in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
.
By representing negation directly, it also severely enlarges the size of the set of
nef
terms (a syntactic notion, not dependent on assigned types) to those
really
not representing negation.
All these are important issues for the creation of theorem provers for Classical Logic.

Our motivation for our work was two-fold: enlarge the set of
nef
-terms, and the fact that (implicative)
{\lambda\mu}
does not fully represent negation.
It is now fair to ask: “Is
{\mathcal{L}}
the finished product?”
In other words, can all logical connectors be expressed in
{\mathcal{L}}
?
Although it is well known that conjunction
A\mathbin{\wedge}B
can be expressed through
\neg(A\mathop{\rightarrow}\neg B)
and disjunction
A\mathbin{\vee}B
through
\neg A\mathord{\rightarrow}B
, this is not enough, since it does not answer the question of representability.
In particular, it seems that rule
({\vee}\textsl{E})
cannot be represented in
\mathrel{\vdash_{\kern-2.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\scriptsize\sc ni}}}
, since that would require a combination of a limited version of
LEM
and
({\vee}\textsl{E})
through the rule

\penalty{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,A}\mathrel{\vdash}{C}\quad{\Gamma\kern-0.975pt,\neg A}\mathrel{\vdash}{C}

{\Gamma}\mathrel{\vdash}{C}

We will leave this issue for future work, as well as the study of
cbv
reduction for
{\mathcal{L}}
.
